# Ear Clef — Project Handoff
**Prepared July 2026 · One Sky Ally / Stefano Kajatt**
*Drop this document into the Ear Clef project knowledge. It contains everything needed to resume from zero context: the full original vision, the 2026 strategic reframe, current assets, and the build plan.*

---

## 1. What this is

Ear Clef (originally **LeeP**) is Stefano's concept for a values-aligned music platform — "the FIOH before FIOH." Developed extensively around 2023 (documented in a long ChatGPT session, summarized below), then back-burnered because the full vision felt too big to start solo. In July 2026 it was strategically re-scoped: **keep everything except hosting/streaming music**, becoming a one-stop artist hub that aggregates rather than hosts.

The founding insight, in Stefano's words: *there is no ultimate music destination.* To watch an artist's videos you go to YouTube; setlists and tour history, setlist.fm; their story, Wikipedia; tickets, Ticketmaster; merch, their website. Streaming services claim to be the destination but they only hold one piece. Ear Clef merges the fan experience cleanly in one place.

Core values: artist-first economics, genuine fan connection over algorithmic noise, transparency, and releasing good ideas into the world even without profit.

---

## 2. The original LeeP vision (2023) — full summary

### Purpose
Fix two problems: (1) an industry where most artists can't make a living; (2) no direct, comfortable channel between artists and fans. Plus the one-stop-shop insight above.

### Three account types
- **Artists** — upload any content in any medium; control what's fans-only vs. public; per-member logins with role restrictions; messaging for band/manager/booking.
- **Fans** — free tier streams everything; a music-only social feed (no distractions); playlists, radio, merch, news, events. Paid "super fan" tier per artist.
- **Agents/promoters** — pay for deep performance data: attendance, ticket prices, marketing spend, merch sold, setlists, venue quality, even weather and parking. A data product nobody else offers.

### The membership model (the crown jewel)
- Fans subscribe to an artist for ~$50/year (6 months of guaranteed weekly exclusive content, then **6 months off for the artist** — sustainability built into the business model; a rhythm designed by someone who understands burnout).
- Perk menu: weekly exclusives (demos, b-sides, lessons, behind-the-scenes), early album access, stems for remixing, tablature and how-to-play videos by the writer, 20–30% merch discounts, presales and early entry, live stream + download of shows, fan-made merch with artist approval and profit splits, contests (flyers, remixes, videos) with real prizes.
- Loyalty ladder: multi-year prepays and 3-year rewards (discounts, VIP tickets, signed vinyl).
- **Band bundles**: artists team up as de facto labels — shared subscriptions, cross-promotion, discovery for smaller acts.
- Economics: 5K fans ≈ $250K/yr for a band (≈$40K/member after expenses and taxes for a 4-piece) — a base income floor before tickets, merch, or licensing.

### Platform revenue (sliding scale, artist-protective)
- 0.01%–3% of artist subscription revenue (bigger artists pay a higher %, small artists nearly nothing)
- Licensing marketplace: artists list music for film/TV/games; verified-buyer subscriptions with purchase-history transparency; small residual to platform
- PPV live streams (5–20%)
- Promoter/agent data subscriptions
- "Band boosts": 3 free algorithmic promotions per year for artists meeting engagement criteria
- Platform merch as a support-the-artists movement symbol

### Recommendation algorithm concept
A famously exhaustive dimension list (100+ musical attributes: instrumentation, harmonic relationships, production techniques, lyric devices, recording gear, context of use, and dozens more) to power recommendations beyond genre matching — including *when* users are most receptive to new music.

### From the original roadmap
- Feature priorities (2023 ranking): artist–fan communication and content hosting were "high priority, high complexity" — **note: the 2026 reframe deletes the highest-complexity item (hosting/streaming) entirely.**
- Competitors analyzed: Patreon, Bandcamp, Spotify, Twitch, OnlyFans. LeeP's differentiators: comprehensiveness, loyalty rewards, promoter data, artist-first fee structure.
- Funding paths sketched: bootstrap → crowdfunding → angels; two crowdfunding video scripts written (one populist, one Apple-minimalist).
- The logo was conceived in this chat: yin-yang with treble clef and ear replacing the dots.

---

## 3. The 2026 strategic reframe (decided July 2026)

**The wall:** hosting music means licensing. Royalty economics are why even Spotify struggles toward profit, and why nobody has merged everything — it's not a lack of imagination, it's rights. The 2023 vision's "free streaming for everyone" is the one unbuildable piece.

**The unlock:** the ultimate music destination doesn't need to host music. **Streaming is the commodity; context is the product.** Precedent: Letterboxd became the cultural home of film without streaming a single movie.

**Ear Clef v-next = the artist-universe hub.** Every artist gets one page that aggregates their whole world: links to the music (Spotify/Apple/Bandcamp), videos, setlists and tour history, upcoming shows and tickets, their story, merch links, community. Pure metadata and links — no licensing, no royalty wall, buildable small.

**What survives from 2023:** the fan-first values, the music-only feed concept, artist-approved fan creativity, the promoter data product (later phase), the 6-on/6-off cadence (as philosophy and manifesto material), band bundles (later phase), the logo.
**What's cut:** hosting audio, streaming, downloads, PPV infrastructure — anything that touches rights.

**Convergence note:** a Vegas-first pilot overlaps naturally with Stefano's Live Now / LVTV concept (live map of Las Vegas entertainment). One project can wear both ideas.

---

## 4. Current assets & status

- **Name:** Ear Clef. No entity or trademark filed (would live under One Sky Ally LLC). Not yet used in commerce.
- **Logo:** yin-yang — treble clef in white half, ear in black half, and the full circle doubles as a head in profile (nose/lips on left edge). Original hand-drawn on paper (photographed; keep and date the paper original — it's authorship evidence). Digital version made in Canva. **Caveat:** if any Canva stock elements were used (the clef may be a standard glyph), Canva's license does not permit trademarking designs with their stock content. Action: redraw/vectorize from the paper original — better art, zero licensing questions. Deliverables when redrawn: SVG master, one-color version, app-icon crop.
- **Companion mark:** treble clef with a padlock whose keyhole is a $ sign — the protest image. Role: statement/campaign graphic; the yin-yang is the identity.
- **Tagline:** "Your Ultimate Music Destination" — flagged as generic; candidates in the spirit of the mark: "Music in balance," "Hear both sides," or none.
- **Source docs:** full ChatGPT chat export (LeeP), Smart Slides deck, Idea Bible entries.

---

## 5. The action ladder (agreed July 2026)

1. **Now / any low-energy week:** redraw logo from paper original → release as art (stickers/prints/shirt). Establishes the mark in commerce cheaply. No downside in any future.
2. **Next:** manifesto essay — *"There Is No Ultimate Music Destination."* Lead with the fragmentation argument, present the vision, explicitly give the idea away. Feature the 6-on/6-off artist cadence. Value: public timestamp under Stefano's name, positioning in the artist-rights conversation, collaborator magnet. Expectation-setting: platforms rarely adopt ideas from blogs and mostly can't accept unsolicited submissions — the essay's return is positioning and community, not acquisition.
3. **Then (post-FIOH launch or genuinely free bandwidth):** v1 pilot — see build plan below.
4. **Never (2026):** full-platform build, private pitches to majors, anything that steals deep-work blocks from FIOH before Nov 1–2.

---

## 6. v1 build plan (for Claude Code)

**Scope discipline is the whole game.** v1 = ONE artist's page, fully working, beautiful. Not "the platform."

**v1 definition of done:** a deployed public page for one artist (Trust in the Sun / Aplete as guinea pig) showing: bio/story · streaming links · embedded/linked videos · upcoming shows · past setlists · merch links · press/news links. Data auto-refreshed where APIs allow, hand-entered where they don't.

**Suggested stack:** Next.js (or similar React framework) · deployed on Netlify (already connected to Stefano's Claude account) · start with a simple JSON/flat-file data layer per artist; add a database only when there are multiple artists.

**Data sources to integrate (register for keys as needed):**
- Spotify Web API — artist metadata, discography, links (free dev tier; quota extension needs approval)
- YouTube Data API — channel/videos
- setlist.fm API — setlists and gig history (free key, easy)
- Ticketmaster Discovery API — upcoming events (free tier)
- Bandsintown — note: their public API is partner-restricted; may need manual entry or alternatives (Songkick API access is also limited) — treat tour data as possibly manual in v1
- Merch/story: manual links in v1

**Phases after v1:** (2) templatize → pages for Boko's circle / Vegas artists, hand-curated; (3) artist self-serve editing + accounts; (4) the music-only feed; (5) promoter data product. Each phase is a separate decision — do not let Claude Code cascade into them uninvited.

**Kickoff prompt sketch for the new chat / Claude Code:**
"Read the Ear Clef handoff doc. Build v1 exactly as scoped in section 6: a single-artist hub page for Trust in the Sun, Next.js, deployed to Netlify. Start with hardcoded/manual data for every section so the page is complete on day one; then wire up setlist.fm and Spotify APIs to replace manual data where possible. Do not build accounts, feeds, or multi-artist features. Show me the page after each milestone."

Lesson: the gatherer now marks an artist `incomplete` when any fetch fails mid-gather and redoes it on the next pass — missing records must never read as "no records" (lesson 5). The restart wrapper stops on a clean exit, so a final `node scripts/gather-extra-play-evidence.mjs` pass is needed after SWEEP-COMPLETE to redo incompletes.
  - **PRECISION FIXES FOUND IN REVIEW OF THE FULL RUN (all local, no refetch):** (1) SPLIT RECORDS leaked through the shared-credit anchor — Linda Vera / Carmen Rivero on one LP served Carmen's video; a shared credit on a record WITH per-track artist credits now falls to the track-level rule (the owner's approved "shared" class is bands-with-singers and duets, i.e. records WITHOUT per-track credits). (2) FEATURED credits accepted ANY Discogs extra role — 162 serving links rested on Written-By / Producer / Arranged By / Remix, i.e. someone else's recording of the artist's work; now PERFORMING roles only, non-performing HELD (`non-performing-credit`). (3) A "<Someone Else> - Topic" channel is YouTube's own statement of whose recording it is — when that someone is not an alias, the video is HELD (`topic-channel-names-another-artist`, 140). (4) "credited on a track but the title matches no track" was REFUTED; it is ambiguous, so now HELD (`track-credit-without-title-match`, 220).
  - **RESULT:** quarantined 3,861 → **759 verified · 1,317 replaced · 1,136 refuted · 649 held (54% serve)**; kept 2,922 → **2,201 verified · 334 replaced · 14 refuted · 373 held (87% serve; 387 go dark)**. **Serving 2,922 → 4,611.** Serving anchors: whole 3,165 · shared 958 · track 274 · featured 214; corroboration legs on serving links: title 4,905 · duration 1,458 · Topic 612 · MB title 207; duration-only 62. Stored-video causes in the quarantine: **master-id collision 2,024 (52%)**, title matches another artist's track 109, no credit 20. Biggest gains: TT 353→643, GH 171→329, CD 258→412, UZ 42→186, HT 93→227, VN 86→194, NI 96→197. Five countries LOSE links under the bar: FJ 10→8, VU 4→3, DJ 2→1, TD 2→1, SO 1→0.

Spot-check of the whole-credit class (10/10 looked right-artist): "Sugus - Wicked Got To Pay + Dub" vs record "Wicked Man Got To Pay"; "Miss Regina Perera ~ Daruwa Welasanin Nida (1928)" vs "Dharuwa Welasanin" — exact-bar misses on transliteration, the artist's OWN record.

(e) FEATURED = performing roles only (my default; 214 serve, 166 non-performing held) — confirm or widen. Then STEP 3: `node scripts/apply-extra-play-identity.mjs --write` (dry run verified: 2,960 verified + 1,651 replaced applied, 387 kept links go dark per the standing rule, `previousPlay` kept on every replacement), accessor unchanged, commit + ONE push, deploy verify, then the queue-gate ruling.
- **GAP-FILL PLAY REPAIR — STEP 1 (SMOKE) DONE (Sep 3, 2026):**
  - **SECOND ROOT CAUSE FOUND, AND IT IS THE BIGGER ONE.** `/artists/{id}/releases` rows can be MASTERS, and `build-extra-play.mjs` fetched their ids as `/releases/{id}` — a different number space — so the walk landed on UNRELATED records: Rage Mob (PY) → Erasmo Carlos "A Carta" 1966; Zambia's "Tiyende Pamodzi" (Kenneth Kaunda, 1974) → a 2005 Superimposers single. A 14-link replay split 9 master-id / 4 compilation / 1 correct; the smoke measured **58% of quarantined stored videos as this collision**. It also STARVED the kept and null buckets — when the mis-fetched release had no videos the walk moved on and the artist's real master videos were never examined — so the 11,197 Discogs-keyed nulls are a phase-2 candidate with the corrected walk (owner sees phase-1 yield first).
  - **BUILT:** `scripts/lib/extraPlayIdentity.mjs` (exact whole-unit title VARIANTS — "A = B" script sides, "A / B" sides, bracket groups, trailing feat., alias at a boundary, 2+-space separators; alias alone is never a title), `scripts/gather-extra-play-evidence.mjs` (decides nothing: Discogs profile + corrected record walk with masters via /masters and main release when the master has no videos, structured credits INCLUDING extraartists, tracklists with per-track credits + durations, attached videos; bounded replay of the master-id bug so collisions are REFUTED with evidence rather than left "unlocated"; YouTube snippet/status/duration, 1 unit per 50 ids; local MB crosswalk by Discogs id → release-group + tracklist titles + channel ids; cache = one JSON per artist in gitignored `data/extra-play-evidence/`), `scripts/arbitrate-extra-play-identity.mjs` (local, re-runnable; report only — the dataset is untouched until the apply step).

REFUTED = the video sits only on records where the artist has no credit (master-id collision, various-artists, or title matches a track credited to someone else). HELD = anchor without corroboration, or not locatable. Artist verdict: stored verified → verified; else best verified candidate (playable, ≥90 s, whole > track > shared > featured > channel) → replaced; else refuted / held. NOTHING IS DELETED — every verdict keeps the stored URL; the apply step will keep a replaced link's old URL as `previousPlay`.

Maloba, The Golden Stones" read as uncredited. Shared credit is now its own anchor class and reported separately so the owner can rule on it. Standing lesson 1 in mirror image: a strict test that fails the truth is as wrong as a loose one that passes a lie.

- **LIKED SONGS — PHASE D (the archive card at /a/[mbid]) BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE; THE WHOLE FEATURE IS DONE (Sep 2, 2026):**
  - **Shipped:** `app/api/explore/artist/[mbid]/route.ts` (new — MB core facts), `app/a/[mbid]/page.tsx` (new), `components/archive/ArchiveArtist.tsx` + `.module.css` (new); the radar and the saved-songs list now link non-roster artists to their card.
  - **NOT ONE WORD IS GENERATED.** The card states what sources record and nothing else: name, MusicBrainz's own disambiguation comment, person/group, origin (begin-area + country), life-span, genre labels, the dated catalogue, and outbound links. There is no biography because there is no biography to verify. Owner's rule, followed literally.
  - **THE GLOBE LINK IS THE PAGE'S REASON TO EXIST, so it is the first thing under the name** — a full-width card, not a footnote: "Hear the scene · Mali, 1976 · Everyone else making music around them". Nothing else on the web can put an artist back among their contemporaries. The year is EARNED, never guessed: their earliest dated release (era-corrected) first, life-span begin as fallback, and where neither exists the card links to the country alone and SAYS why ("no dated record here to pin a year to").
  - **A rostered artist can never land here** — `page.tsx` looks up the MBID against `getAllArtists()` and 307s to `/[slug]`. Two pages for one artist would split what 130 hand-verified pages earned. Verified: `/a/a74b1b7f…` → 307 → `/radiohead`.
  - **`robots: noindex`** (follow still on). Letting thin auto-assembled cards compete in search against the hand-verified roster would devalue the ones that took the work. One line to reverse if the owner ever wants them discoverable.
  - **Visually distinct BY CONSTRUCTION:** no hero art, no story, no merch, no press. Squared corners (3–4px, against the roster's rounded language), uppercase letter-spaced micro-labels, a dossier fact-grid. The overline reads "ARCHIVE CARD" and the footer says plainly that Ear Clef's own artist pages are hand-verified and look nothing like this one.
  - **Playback costs ZERO new quota.** The card never resolves videos — it plays the LISTENER'S OWN saved songs by that artist (already playability-verified when liked) through `QueuePlayer`'s `preresolved`. Calling the queue resolver here would have spent YouTube quota on every visit of every card.
  - **Reused rather than rebuilt:** `artist-era` (era-corrected catalogue, `originalSpan`/`editionYear` rendered) and `artist-links` — which deliberately refuses official homepages after the Aug 29 expired-domain incident, so this card inherits that protection instead of reintroducing the hole.
  - **Verified live against real MusicBrainz data** (Ali Farka Touré, `9be2a5ac-8201-489b-b5f6-91f958bf9060`): renders Tombouctou/Mali, 1939–2006, Person, three genres, 20 of 21 releases dated from 1976, Spotify + Apple Music. **The scene card was CLICKED through** and landed on `/?y=1976&c=ML` with the globe showing "Top artists from Mali · 180". Malformed id → 404; rostered id → redirect; a saved song for the artist surfaced "▶ Play your 1 saved song". Console clean. Error states tell three truths apart (no such artist / source busy / unreachable) — only the first is permanent.
  - **Known limits, stated not hidden:** the catalogue sweep is capped (`truncated` is surfaced honestly); there is no cover art yet (CAA was scoped but not built — the card is text-only for now); and MusicBrainz throttles, so a card can legitimately need a retry.

- **LIKED SONGS — C1 (the derived radar tier) BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE; PHASES A–C COMPLETE (Sep 2, 2026, committed + pushed as a4fd15d):**
  - **Shipped:** `lib/fans/radar.ts` (new — pure derivation), `components/fans/RadarFromLikes.tsx` + `.module.css` (new), `radarDismissed` through store/API/provider/localStorage, and `TasteMap` now takes `rosterByMbid`, computes the radar and renders it INSIDE its existing "On the radar" group.
  - **THE TIER IS DERIVED, NEVER STORED.** Liking a song puts its artist on the third tier by COMPUTATION on every render — the only radar state written anywhere is the list of explicit dismissals. This is what stops the drift a stored tier would guarantee: un-like the last song by someone and they leave the radar on their own, with no second record to keep in step. Verified live: un-liking one of two songs dropped the count 2→1, un-liking the second removed the artist entirely.
  - **Identity is the MBID and nothing else.** MBID-less (#1-hits) likes can never put anyone on the radar, and rather than let that look broken, the count is DISCLOSED: "N saved songs came from a chart queue, which carries no artist id — a name alone isn't enough to put someone here." Never a name match (standing lessons 1/7).
  - **The radar never shadows the map proper:** an artist the fan already FOLLOWS is excluded from the derived list, because they are already placed under whatever tier the fan chose. Verified: one-tap Follow moved Radiohead out of the radar into "Following · 1" with a freshly minted Fan #1, in one click.
  - **A dismissal must have a way back.** The first cut had a × with no undo — one tap silently and permanently hiding an artist the listener still has songs by, and it left the provider's restore branch dead. Added "N artists hidden from your radar · Show them again", offering ONLY dismissed artists still behind saved songs (`dismissedMbids`); a stale id from a since-un-liked song is not offered, because restoring someone who would not reappear is a lie. Dismissals persist server-side AND locally, and never touch the saved songs themselves (verified: 4 likes intact after a dismiss).
  - **JSX GOTCHA WORTH KEEPING (cost a real render bug here).** `{expr} word` renders as `{expr}word` — the leading space is LOST — whenever that text node spans multiple lines; on a single line it survives. This shipped "1 saved songcame from a chart queue" to the screen. That is exactly why this codebase writes `{' '}` explicitly everywhere. When an expression is followed by prose that wraps, use `{' '}` or start the string literal with its own space.
  - **Verified live end to end:** radar renders with ZERO follows (the taste map's empty state no longer swallows it); sorted by song count; Follow shown only where a roster page exists; dismiss → persists across reload via the record; restore brings the artist back and clears the server list; the "· 0" count is suppressed when only the disclosure note remains. Console clean but for the localhost Cloudflare-CORS noise. All test state cleared afterwards.
  - **NEXT:** Phase D (its own go, per the owner) — the light artist page at `/a/[mbid]` for liked non-roster artists. Everything it may honestly contain, and the hard rule that it must NOT live in the `/[slug]` roster namespace, is in the Sep 1 design entry below.

- **LIKED SONGS — B2 (play-all + the dead-video skip) BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE; PHASE B COMPLETE (Sep 2, 2026, committed + pushed as abcb26b):**
  - **Shipped:** `QueuePlayer` gains an `onError` handler, a dead-video set, and an optional `endNote` prop; `LikedSongs` renders a `QueuePlayer` over the FILTERED set via `preresolved`.
  - **THE BUG THIS CHUNK EXISTED TO FIX (and it was live in the hits queues all along).** `onStateChange` only ever handled `ENDED` — but **ENDED never fires for a video that never started**. One video pulled, made private, or embedding-disabled since the day it was verified would stall a queue FOREVER, with no error and no advance. `onError` now marks it and jumps to the next. This was never a saved-songs-only problem: it silently threatened every #1-hits queue on the site, and every place+era queue whose cached resolve has since rotted.
  - **Dead videos are SKIPPED, NOT REMOVED** (the owner's no-deletion rule, applied to playback): the row stays in the queue marked `⊘` with its play button disabled and an honest title, `seekIncluded` steps over it, and the end line adds "N tracks are no longer playable on YouTube — skipped, not removed." A saved song silently vanishing is worse than one that says why. The dead set lives in a REF (the seek reads it synchronously, mid-callback) AND state (the list renders it).
  - **`endNote` exists because a pre-resolved queue is `exhausted` from the moment it starts** — so its end line is on screen while track 1 is still playing. That is why the wording is "That's everything saved in this queue" (completeness) and NOT "that's the end" (completion). The place+era default is untouched.
  - **The queue is built from what is SHOWN, at click time, and is deliberately NOT re-keyed on the filters** — the same contract CountryPanel has. Verified live: changing a chip mid-song left the SAME iframe playing the SAME track while the list below updated to "2 of 3".
  - **Verified live on /me** with a seeded set whose FIRST track is a deliberately dead video id: play-all started, YouTube fired onError, the track was marked `⊘` + disabled, the queue advanced to track 2 and actually played it, and the note read "That's everything saved in this queue. One track is no longer playable on YouTube — skipped, not removed." Filtered play relabels to "▶ Play these 2" and queues exactly those two. **Regression-checked the hits queue** (`/?y=1971&c=GB`): unchanged, still shows its own "Only 2 verified tracks here — honest, not padded." Console clean of everything but the localhost Cloudflare-CORS noise.
  - **Small gap, consistent with the rest of the site:** once a queue is playing the play button is gone, so filtering afterwards re-filters the LIST but does not offer to rebuild the queue. CountryPanel behaves the same way. Not worth a rebuild prompt unless it annoys in use.
  - **NEXT:** C1 — the derived radar tier on the taste map: distinct MBIDs from the likes, minus a dismissed list, with one-tap Follow beside roster artists. Remember MBID-less (#1-hits) likes can NEVER put an artist on the radar, and the UI must say so rather than name-match.

- **LIKED SONGS — B1 (the /me playlist) BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE (Sep 2, 2026, committed + pushed as d64fcac):**
  - **Shipped:** `components/fans/LikedSongs.tsx` + `.module.css` (new — the saved-songs section), `signedIn` exposed from `LikesProvider`, and `app/me/page.tsx` now also builds an MBID→roster map (same construction as `app/page.tsx`) and renders `<LikedSongs>` under the taste map.
  - **PLACE LABELS — the naming question settled with a live probe, not an assumption.** The A2 entry flagged that a `?c=GB` deep link could store `placeName: "GB"`. Probed both paths: with the globe ON, the deep link resolves properly to "United Kingdom" (GlobeScene supplies `feature.properties.ADMIN`); the code-as-name happens ONLY in the no-WebGL fallback, which is where the A2 test ran. So stored names are trustworthy in real use, and `placeLabel()` simply prefers `placeName`, falling back to `placeCode` when the name is missing or IS the code. **No 250-country name map was shipped** — the globe's own name source is a 215KB geojson, far too heavy to load on /me for cosmetics. Filtering always groups by the LABEL, so it stays correct either way.
  - **Chips follow the house `TierFilter` pattern** (single-select pills with an "All" default, hidden when there are fewer than 2 choices), one row for genre and one for place, sorted by count then name, each showing its count. The two combine with AND, and an impossible combination gets an honest "Nothing saved matches both filters" + Clear filters — never a silently blank list.
  - **Roster linking is by MBID and nothing else.** A liked artist the site has a page for links to it; everyone else is plain text. No name matching — a bare artist name is not an identity (standing lessons 1/7), and hits-queue likes carry no MBID by construction.
  - **Verified live on /me** with a seeded 6-song set (varied genres, 5 places, one real roster MBID): renders newest-first with counts; genre chips → 2 of 6; + place chip → 1 of 6 (AND holds); impossible pair → honest empty state; Clear filters restores 6; un-♥ removes the row, updates localStorage AND recomputes the chips (alternative rock vanished with its only track); Radiohead linked to /radiohead while non-roster artists stayed plain; signed-out note reads "Saved in this browser only — sign in to keep them across devices"; empty state hides both chip rows. Console clean apart from the localhost Cloudflare-CORS noise and the signed-out 401.
  - **Screenshot recipe that WORKS for a below-the-fold section** (the body-transform trick in the operational notes produced a black frame here): temporarily `display:none` the section's siblings so it lands at scroll 0, screenshot, then reload. 
  - **NEXT:** B2 — Play all from /me via `QueuePlayer`'s `preresolved` prop (respecting the active filters), plus the player's missing `onError` → skip-to-next path, without which one dead video stalls a whole playlist (it also protects the existing hits queues).

- **LIKED SONGS — A3 (the sign-in merge) BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE; PHASE A COMPLETE (Sep 2, 2026, committed + pushed as e2712c4):**
  - **Shipped:** `lib/fans/likesStorage.ts` gains a SECOND list (`earclef_likes_pending`); `components/fans/LikesProvider.tsx` merges it on mount and keeps it honest on every toggle.
  - **THE BUG THIS DESIGN EXISTS TO PREVENT — RESURRECTING THE DEAD.** The obvious A3 ("on sign-in, POST the browser's likes") is WRONG, because after A2 localStorage is also the offline MIRROR of the server list. Merging the mirror means: like a song, unlike it later on your phone, and the laptop's stale mirror pushes it straight back on the next load — a deliberate removal silently undone. So the browser keeps TWO lists with different jobs: the CACHE (what to paint immediately; the whole truth while signed out) and PENDING (only likes the server has never confirmed). **Only PENDING is ever merged.** A like the record has acknowledged is never pending, so it is never re-sent, so a removal stays removed.
  - **Pending is queued FIRST and cleared only on confirmation.** `toggleLike` adds to pending before the POST and drops it only when the record answers ok — so a like made signed out, or one whose POST never landed (offline, flaky network), is exactly what the next signed-in load hands over. An `atCapacity` refusal does NOT queue: retrying would only be refused again. An UNLIKE also clears any queued copy, so a song the listener just removed can never be handed over by a later merge.
  - **After the merge, anything still pending genuinely did not make it** (the shelf was full), so it stays queued for the next attempt AND stays VISIBLE via `unionLikes(server, pending)` — never silently dropped. `skipped > 0` raises the same honest capacity note A2 built.
  - **Verified live, four scenarios, all pass:** (1) two likes made SIGNED OUT queue as pending with the server untouched, then land on the record with full context the moment a session appears — verified on the /me load, since the provider is in the layout and merges from any page; (2) **no resurrection** — with the cache holding two songs, one was unliked from a SECOND device (a curl session on the same account); after reload the browser showed only the surviving song and did NOT push the removed one back; (3) a like whose POST was forced to fail (fetch monkey-patched to reject POSTs) stayed pending with the ♥ still lit, and SELF-HEALED onto the record on the next load; (4) console clean — only the localhost Cloudflare-analytics CORS noise.
  - **Known gap, deliberately not built:** a failed UNLIKE has no pending queue of its own, so if that POST dies the song returns on the next load (server truth wins). Recoverable by unliking again; a full two-way sync is not worth its complexity here. Flagged rather than hidden.
  - **NEXT:** B1 — the /me liked-songs section (list, un-♥, genre + place chips). Resolve the place label from the stored `placeCode`, `placeName` only as fallback (see the deep-link naming quirk in the A2 entry).

- **LIKED SONGS — A2 (the ♥ control) BUILT AND VERIFIED LIVE IN THE PLAYER (Sep 2, 2026, committed + pushed as 7997bed):**
  - **Shipped:** `components/fans/LikesProvider.tsx` (new — site-wide likes context, mounted in `app/layout.tsx` inside `ServiceProvider`), `components/fans/LikeButton.tsx` + `.module.css` (new — the ♥, coral `#ff7a6b` against the queue's amber, `now` and `row` variants), `lib/fans/likesStorage.ts` (new — the localStorage layer), `unionLikes` restored to `lib/fans/likes.ts` (it now has a real caller), and `placeCode` threaded into `QueuePlayer` from BOTH call sites.
  - **A2 turned out to be local-first already, because that IS the house pattern.** `ServiceProvider` (the listen-service picker) reads localStorage AND the fan record on mount and writes both on change; `LikesProvider` is built to the same shape, so the ♥ works signed out from day one. What A3 still owes is only the SIGN-IN MERGE — POSTing `mergeLikes` so browser-only likes become durable server likes.
  - **A signed-in fan sees `unionLikes(server, local)`** — server wins a collision (it holds the context the song was FIRST liked in). This exists so nothing appears to VANISH on sign-in: without it, likes made signed-out would disappear from view the moment you signed in, until A3 landed. Verified live: signed in against an EMPTY server record, the locally-liked track still read as ♥.
  - **The ♥ never waits on the network.** `toggleLike` commits to state + localStorage immediately and POSTs in the background; a 401 is not a failure (it just means nobody is signed in). The ONE thing that can take a ♥ back is the server answering `atCapacity` — the shelf is full, the save was refused, and showing a like that is not really there would be a lie. That path renders an honest note in the player pointing at /me to make room.
  - **The ♥ is DISABLED until stored likes are read** (`ready`). Rendering ♡ for a song that is actually saved is a lie the user would act on; a brief disabled beat is not.
  - **Lint fix worth remembering:** `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rejects a synchronous `setState` in an effect body. `ServiceProvider` gets away with its pattern because every `setState` sits AFTER an `await`. `LikesProvider` now reads localStorage inside the async IIFE for the same reason, and the profile fetch carries `AbortSignal.timeout(8000)` so a hung request can never leave every ♥ permanently disabled.
  - **Verified live in the browser pane** on `/?y=1971&c=GB&noglobe=1` (the #1-hits queue is PRE-RESOLVED, so this whole verification cost ZERO YouTube quota — use it for any future player UI work): ♥ appears in the now-playing bar and on every queue row; clicking one flips BOTH the row and the now-playing ♥ (shared context); the like stores `{videoId, title, artistName, placeCode: 'GB', year: 1971}` with NO mbid (correct — hits tracks carry none); it survives a full page reload; signed in, a like round-trips to `/api/fan` and an unlike removes it from server AND localStorage; cleared storage renders all ♡. No React or hydration errors — console holds only the expected Cloudflare-analytics CORS noise on localhost and the 401 from the signed-out POST.
  - **Known display quirk (PRE-EXISTING, not from this work):** the deep-link path sets `name: pendingCountry.current` (ExploreClient.tsx:279), so `/?c=GB` names the panel "GB" instead of "United Kingdom" — a normal country CLICK names it properly. Consequence for B1: resolve the playlist's place label from the stored `placeCode`, with `placeName` only as fallback, rather than trusting the stored name.
  - **NEXT:** A3 — POST `mergeLikes` when a session appears, then drop the merged entries from localStorage; surface `skipped` honestly ("N didn't fit — still saved in this browser").

- **LIKED SONGS — A1 (the model) BUILT AND VERIFIED, COMMITTED + PUSHED as 250564a (Sep 1, 2026):**
  - **Owner-approved design (propose-then-build, Sep 1):** likes are LOCAL-FIRST (♥ works signed out, merges into the fan record on sign-in); the radar tier is DERIVED from liked artists, never stored, with one-tap follow beside each; Phase D (a light artist page for liked non-roster artists) is in scope but takes its OWN go after A–C land.
  - **Phase order:** A1 model ✅ · A2 ♥ in QueuePlayer + place-code prop from both call sites · A3 local-first + sign-in merge · B1 /me liked-songs section with genre+place chips · B2 play-all via `preresolved` + player onError-skip · C1 derived radar · D archive card at `/a/[mbid]` (separate go).
  - **A1 shipped in code (not committed, not pushed):** `lib/fans/likes.ts` (new — the `LikedTrack` shape and the ONLY gate anything enters it through; pure and dependency-free so the client holds the same shape in localStorage), `lib/fans/store.ts` (`likes` on `FanRecord`, `addLike`/`removeLike`/`mergeLikes`), `app/api/fan/route.ts` (GET returns `likes`; POST accepts `like` / `unlike` / `mergeLikes`).
  - **A like stores the track AS THE QUEUE VERIFIED IT:** videoId (playability-checked by the resolver chain before a listener ever heard it, so a replayed like is a verified destination, never a search URL), title, artistName, and — frozen at like time — the artist's genre tags plus the place+era the queue was walking. Frozen because the pools churn: a widen empties and refills them, and a saved song must not lose the labels its own filters are built on.
  - **The likes branches sit BEFORE the roster-slug check in the route** — most of what a listener saves out of a place-and-era queue is an artist the site has no page for, and that is the point of the feature.
  - **#1-hits likes carry no MBID and that is load-bearing:** `HitsSection` builds `preresolved` tracks with `mbid: ''`, and a bare name is not an identity (standing lesson 1/7). Those likes save the SONG fine but can never put an artist on the radar; C1 derives the radar from MBIDs only, and the UI must say so rather than name-match.
  - **NOTHING IS EVER EVICTED (the owner's no-deletion ruling, applied to a cap).** `MAX_LIKES` = 500. A like past the cap is REFUSED with `atCapacity: true` — the listener is told, never silently overruled — and `mergeLikes` offers only the free space below the cap to newcomers, newest first, so a sign-in merge can never evict a like already on the server; whatever does not fit comes back as `skipped` and stays in the browser. Every optional field is DROPPED rather than stored malformed (bad MBID, out-of-range year, path-traversal placeCode), and a client `likedAt` is honoured (it preserves the order of signed-out likes) but clamped to now, so a skewed clock cannot pin a track to the top forever.
  - **Verified against a real dev server, 45 assertions, all pass** (scripts in the session scratchpad: `a1-check.mjs` 24, `a1-cap-check.mjs` 10, `a1-crossfield-check.mjs` 11). Covered: sanitization of every optional field, duplicate likes idempotent with the ORIGINAL timestamp kept, malformed input refused 400, MBID-less hits likes saved, newest-first ordering, unlike (incl. no-op on an unsaved song), the merge's added/skipped/rejected counts, signed-out writes rejected 401, the cap refusing without evicting, a freed slot accepting a new like, and likes surviving follow/tier/service/share mutations (and vice versa — `baseRecord` carries `likes` through).
  - **Dev-store caveat:** with no NETLIFY env keys and no `.netlify` blob dir locally, `getStore` throws and every write lands in the in-process dev Map — ephemeral, and nothing in these runs touched production. The 500-like fill exists only in that dead dev process.
  - **NEXT:** A2 — the ♥ control in `QueuePlayer`, which needs a `placeCode` prop threaded from BOTH call sites (`CountryPanel.tsx` has `country.code`, `HitsSection.tsx` has `countryCode`); the player currently receives `placeName` only.

- **ERA-DATING BUG — OWNER RULINGS LANDED, RE-DATING SWEEP SCOPED, AWAITING G1 GO (Aug 31, 2026, second session pass):**
  - **Owner rulings (binding):** (1) Nothing is EVER deleted, and nothing is removed or demoted for being wrongly dated — the excluded-compilations Phase 1 is DEAD (probe showed 5/18 vintage artists have nothing else; they'd vanish). (2) Heat map: leave alone, not a priority. (3) The fix is RE-DATING: move wrongly dated music to its true year or as close as evidence allows — same decade is a win; a '60s record surfacing in the '90s is the problem, 1963-for-1958 is fine. (4) Where nothing can be established, the music stays EXACTLY where it is — no moves, no hiding. Signals NOMINATE candidates; only positive external evidence of an older original MOVES a date.
  - **MOVE, DON'T COPY (owner ruling sharpened Aug 31, after the first scope):** once a record is KNOWN to be from 1964 it must no longer be reachable from 1996. A corrected record LEAVES the wrong era entirely and lives only in its real one; uncorrected records stay untouched wherever they are; nothing is deleted. **This is a real change to Stage 4, not a restatement:** under today's `rankEraTitles` a corrected 1964 date would STILL serve 1996, because the `catalog` tier keeps every dated group in the ranked list as a last-resort filler. Implementing the ruling means a corrected record loses catalog-tier service and is eligible only where its TRUE year is in-era or nearby. Uncorrected records keep catalog service, so only artists whose ENTIRE catalogue is corrected can fall silent in a decade.
  - **Blast radius is bounded by the `nearby` band, not by the decade.** `nearby` is ±15y of decade-mid, so a corrected 1964 record still serves the 1950s, 1960s AND 1970s — it stops serving only decades 20+ years away, i.e. the CD era (1985+). The thinning therefore lands exactly where the false padding was.
  - **WIDEN DOES NOT COVER IT (verified in code, not assumed).** CountryPanel.tsx:626 passes `year={year}` — the ORIGINAL year — to QueuePlayer, which computes `decade = Math.floor(year/10)*10`. The widen changes the panel's fetch span (`yearStart`/`yearEnd`) and so the artist POOL, but never the decade the resolver era-picks against. Widening a thin 1996 therefore adds ±5y of artists who are then asked for 1990s tracks they may equally not have. Widen adds ARTISTS, not ERAS — if a decade needs rescuing after correction, the fix has to be a decade-aware widen (pass the span, or let the queue offer the adjacent decade), which is its own proposal.
  - **Cross-referencing proven on the reported case:** Discogs (DISCOGS_TOKEN in .env.local; 60 req/min) holds the original Sondor 7" "Tu Corazon / Brasil" — **1964** — plus a 78rpm shellac; iTunes Search API (free) returns the album with `℗ 1963 SONDOR` machine-readable in `copyright`. Two independent sources, same decade. Discogs is primary; iTunes second-pass (℗ can be a remaster year — iTunes-only evidence must land inside the artist's activity window and ≥10y before the MB date).
  - **Ferrer test (walked on his real 25-group MB catalog):** genuinely-late records are protected structurally. His 1999/2003 Buena Vista-era albums fire no signal (dated within lifetime, Album, no comp tag) → never researched. Posthumous "Mi sueño" (2007) fires the posthumous signal → researched → evidence CONFIRMS 2007 → stays. His comps of 1950s Chepín recordings → researched → move only if original-era records are actually found. Wasted lookups possible (his gap-rule flag nominates the whole catalog); wrong moves are not.
  - **Scoping numbers (probes, Aug 31):** sweep population = 24,172 roster artists (cs≤1975 OR end≤2005). Random-sample probe (40 vintage artists): median 3 / mean 5.3 candidate RGs per artist, 30% have zero, tail is classical + comp-mills (Taizé: 50) → propose classical carve-out (recordings of old works are era-true to the RECORDING; re-dating Vivaldi to 1720 is absurd). Estimated ~50-80k candidate RGs after filters (exact count is a Stage-1 output).
  - **STAGE 1 IS DONE (Aug 31, 2026, from the local dump index — ~1 minute, 0 partial rows). THE G1 CENSUS:** population in scope 24,172; **23,580 swept** (592 skipped as pre-1900 deaths); **15,722 artists carry candidates**; **237,414 CANDIDATE GROUPS**; **88,657 provably displaced 10+ years** (dated after the artist finished — no assumption about the material needed). Buckets: performer 21,667 artists/156,018 cands · **artMusic 1,458 artists/75,853 cands (6% of artists, 32% of volume)** · tradition 97/489 · composer 358/5,054. Signal mix: comp-only 67,867 · **gap-only 50,681 (weakest class)** · gap+posthumous 39,222 · comp+posthumous 36,458 · posthumous-only 23,692 · all-three 12,179 · comp+gap 7,315. Max-plausible displacement: 40+yrs 173,164 · 20–39 53,817 · 10–19 7,486 · 1–9 2,759. Top countries by volume: US 60,863 · GB 30,805 · FR 17,415 · DE 16,710 · AT 10,270 · RU 10,170 · IT 8,918. **Archival-tier 17: only 63 artists / 128 candidates** — MB barely holds them, so re-dating will NOT thin those queues.
  - **STAGE 2 COST IS PER ARTIST, NOT PER CANDIDATE** (one Discogs catalogue search covers all of an artist's candidates; 60 req/min authenticated, ~2 req/artist) — so the earlier "2–5 nights" estimate was pessimistic. Options costed: EVERYTHING 15,722 artists ≈ **8.7h** · minus artMusic 14,369 ≈ 8.0h · minus gap-only 12,556 ≈ 7.0h · minus both 11,335 ≈ 6.3h · **provably-displaced-only 4,811 ≈ 2.7h** · provably-displaced minus artMusic 4,247 ≈ 2.4h · comp-tagged only 11,002 ≈ 6.1h. Even EVERYTHING is one overnight run. **G2 = the owner's ruling on artMusic (Callas's 1950s opera on 90s CD is the real bug; a 1995 recording of a dead composer's symphony is an honest 1995 date — opposite answers in one bucket) and on whether gap-only is worth the lookups.**
  - **STAGE 2 BUILT (Aug 31, owner: "Run everything. No pre-filtering — the arbitration rule already protects us").** `scripts/build-rg-dating-evidence.mjs` + `scripts/lib/mbArtistLinks.mjs` + `scripts/build-mb-artist-index.mjs` (Discogs/YouTube URL relations out of artist.tar.xz, 2 GB — the artist dump also banks the queue resolver's url-rels call for a later session). Gathers evidence ONLY; rules on nothing.
  - **ID-ONLY IDENTITY WOULD HAVE FAILED THE FOUNDING CASE — caught before launch.** Donato Racciatti carries NO Discogs relation in MusicBrainz (only Wikidata), so an id-only sweep would have skipped him and "Tu corazón" would never have been re-dated. Crosswalk coverage is thinnest on exactly the vintage non-Anglo catalogue this site exists for. Identity is therefore TIERED and every match records its tier: `id-crosswalk` (MB's own discogs rel) → `name-unique` (Discogs holds EXACTLY ONE artist of that exact normalised name; two or more = `ambiguous-name`, yields nothing). The owner can later rule `name-unique` in or out without re-running the sweep.
  - **THREE VINTAGE-SPECIFIC DEFECTS FOUND BY TESTING THE FOUNDING CASE, all fixed:** (1) **pre-LP singles are titled "A-side / B-side"** ("Sin Estrellas / Tu Corazon"), so whole-string matching misses every single of the era — each slash-separated side is now its own exact key (still whole-title units, never substring containment). (2) **One artist is often several Discogs entities** — the 1964 original is filed under "Donato Racciatti Y Su Orquesta Típica", NOT the plain "Donato Racciatti" (id 2364880) the id path lands on — so an ADAPTIVE second pass name-searches releases only when candidates remain unmatched. (3) **~half of vintage Discogs entries carry no year at all** (both "Tu Corazon" pressings on his artist page are `????`) — counted per artist as `undatedSeen`; this is the coverage ceiling of the whole stage and must be reported, not discovered later.
  - **CROSSWALK COVERAGE, MEASURED across all 15,722 candidate artists: 87.8% have an ID-LEVEL Discogs relation** (13,803 artists / 208,460 candidates), 810 rejected as ambiguous (2+ relations), and only **12.2% (1,919 artists / 28,954 candidates) fall to the `name-unique` fallback**. Far better than the founding case implied. Weakest id-coverage is exactly where predicted — VN 42.9%, CN 50.0%, BD 54.5%, GE 62.5%, AM 63.3%, ET 66.7%, TW 67.6%, CO 68.7%; strongest LB/PT/NG/LT/ML/HR at 95–97%. Artist index also banks **192,119 YouTube channel relations** — the queue resolver's second per-artist MB call, for a later session.
  - **A BUG I INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT IN REVIEW, worth keeping as the lesson:** the adaptive name-search pass enumerates by NAME, so running it for an `id-crosswalk` artist whose name was never checked for uniqueness could pull a DIFFERENT artist's records into the title index — the John Mayer failure re-entering through a convenience path added after the tiering. The pass is now GATED on name uniqueness regardless of tier (checked lazily, only when the pass is wanted). Not unique = no second pass: less evidence, never wrong evidence.
  - **PACING BELONGS IN ONE PLACE.** Per-call-site sleeps stacked — an artist needing three requests paid four sleeps and ran at 4.8s (≈21h projected). Replaced with a single `paced()` gate that waits only as long as is actually needed since the last request: **3.66s/artist ≈ 16h**. Measured, not estimated.
  - **YIELD, MEASURED BEFORE LAUNCH (12-artist probe + 30-artist UY run): ~10% of candidates find datable evidence.** That is NOT a matching defect — verified by inspecting unmatched titles: they are newly-assembled compilations ("Masterworks From the NCPA Archives", "Maestro's Choice: Series One", "Ru-Ba-Ru Vol.1") with no earlier record bearing that title, exactly the class the owner ruled stays put. Undated Discogs entries ~11%. UY smoke: 26 datable records, **median shift 19 years, max 50, and 21 of 26 cross a decade boundary** — the displacement the owner cares about. ~10% of 237,414 ≈ **24,000 records expected to move**.
  - **STAGE 2 LAUNCHED Aug 31** (`nohup … --minutes 1200`, log data/rg-dating-evidence.log, output data/rg-dating-evidence/<CODE>.json, both gitignored). Resumable and monotonic; re-run to continue after any stop.
  - **OWNER RULE, RESTATED AT DEPTH (Aug 31, replaces the earlier phrasing): MUSIC BELONGS TO WHEN IT WAS CREATED**, not when remastered or re-released. Exception: a SPANNING compilation (60s–90s hits issued in the 90s) has no single true year. Best case is PER-SONG dating — a '50s track lives in the '50s. Fallback if per-song is too complicated: album keeps its release year ONLY if some of its music was actually created then; a pure retrospective belongs to the era it represents.
  - **STAGE 3 BUILT — ARBITRATION (Sep 1, owner: "Go. Formalize Stage 3 around exactly these rules."). `scripts/build-rg-dating-corrections.mjs` → data/rg-dating-corrections.json + data/rg-dating-held.json. Writes corrections only; Stage 4 applies them (own go).** THE PRINCIPLE: a track belongs to the year its RECORDING was made — comp = original recording; live album / re-recording = the performance, so those keep their release year. Rules as built: (1) MB `Live` secondary type → NEVER touched. (2) per-song dating reaches compilations AND posthumous releases only. (3) per-song beats per-album — a dated song uses its own year whatever the album's verdict. (4) the 3-year threshold applies ONLY to songs that could not be dated individually (newest dated track vs release year). (5) no evidence, no move. name-unique additionally must clear career-window overlap.
  - **FOUR DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING IT ON REAL DATA — each one changed the output:**
    1. **Comp-tag-only was too narrow, and the founding case proved it.** Julio Sosa (d. 1964) has 20 candidates of which only 8 are Compilation-tagged; the other 12 are posthumous and UNTAGGED ("30 aniversario 1964-1994", "La historia de Julio Sosa"). Tag-only dating skipped 60% of exactly the records that make Uruguay's 1990s wrong. **Extension: posthumous releases are eligible too — an artist already dead cannot have re-recorded anything, so rule 2's whole purpose (protecting re-recordings) does not apply to them.** A LIVING artist's untagged album still keeps its own date: Racciatti's "Bien milonguero" (1996, he died 2000) stays put — Buena Vista protection intact. Reaches +28,773 candidates.
    2. **Discogs years are RELEASE years, not recording years.** For a dead artist whose every pressing is a posthumous reissue, the earliest pressing still postdates them — a lower BOUND, not a date. It produced visibly impossible spans ("1965–1991" for Sosa). Evidence dated after the artist's death is now rejected outright: **10,111 song datings dropped**, and the survivors are honest.
    3. **Last-writer-wins on SHARED release groups.** A comp credited to several artists was visited once per artist and the last one overwrote the rest — Pontier's unconstrained pass rewrote Sosa's death-constrained span back to impossible. Album verdicts are now accumulated across every credited artist and computed ONCE from the union, each artist's contribution already filtered by their own lifetime.
    4. **Self-audit added:** a span cannot end after every credited artist has finished. 16 of 32,043 (0.05%) still do → `held-impossible`, verdict withheld, surfaced not shipped. (Sosa's remaining over-1964 spans are NOT errors: those comps are shared with Armando Pontier, who lived longer, so a genuinely spanning multi-artist comp legitimately reaches later years.)
  - **STAGE 4 SHIPPED — COMMITTED f14fac2 (Sep 1; owner: "Go. Commit the 8.8 MB as you recommend"). NOT YET PUSHED — push needs the owner's PAT per protocol.** What landed: `scripts/build-rg-dating-apply.mjs` → `lib/explore/rg-dating/` (256 shards + index, 8.8 MB, version 969c119e2667, country-artists pattern) · `lib/explore/rgDating.ts` reader (lazy shard import; `rgDatingTitleKey` MUST stay in sync with the evidence sweep's normalisation — a corrected song is found by this key or not at all) · queue resolver `rankEraTitles` now RANGE-based (per-song year > album span > MB year; corrected titles outside in-era/nearby are DROPPED — move-don't-copy — while uncorrected keep catalog service) · queue cache entries carry `cv` (corrections version): only corrected artists ever revalidate, lazily, no GATE_PASS storm · era panel counts corrected groups ONLY in their true era and renders spans ("1949–1975 · Album · this edition 2011"); method note now reads "By the year the recordings were made…".
  - **STAGE 4 VERIFIED LIVE (dev server + browser pane):** artist-era Racciatti/1996 lists ONLY "Bien milonguero" (Tu corazón has LEFT); /1960-1969 lists "Tu corazón · 1964 · this edition 2001" + spans; queue resolve decade-1990 for Racciatti and Miranda no longer serves Tu corazón; **Miranda decade-1960 serves `[in-era] "Tu Corazón"` — the exact video the owner heard wrongly under Uruguay 1996 now plays where it belongs.** Panel rendering eyeballed in the pane (span + edition + new method note). Racciatti's own 1990s resolve now returns no tracks — correct: the old serving rode Miranda's resolve; his uncorrected "Bien milonguero" anchors his search and finds no verified channel hit, the resolver's pre-existing honest-null.
  - **Committed alongside as ruling-round evidence:** data/rg-dating-held.json (110), data/rg-dating-resolutions.json (18 wikidata-confirmed + 14 quarantined), data/rg-dating-live-review.json (the owner's 96-case list, OPEN — rulings still to come), data/rg-dating-profiles.json. Working data stays gitignored (corrections.json added to .gitignore).

Data-driven assist: 13 rows flagged where the album title literally matches a dated entry in the artist's own catalogue — 3 are clean SONG-name cases (Sandie Shaw "Long Live Love" 1965, Bob Marley "Wake Up and Live" 1979, Q65 "The Life I Live" 1966), the rest reveal an EARLIER PRESSING of the same live album (Neil Diamond "Greatest Hits Live" 1988 vs MB 1994) — which suggests a FUTURE refinement, deliberately not built: live albums re-dated as whole albums to their first pressing (a live album belongs to its release year, and MB's year is sometimes a reissue's). Corrections file after rulings: **167,664 songs across 8,563 artists**; held now exactly 110 = 96 review + 14 quarantined.

Audit re-keyed by MBID; zero remain. (2) **Profile-disagrees 32 → 14:** Wikidata birth/death (P569/P570) used as third-source tie-break — 18 cases CONFIRMED MusicBrainz (profile text was the outlier; identity stands, corrections proceed; recorded in data/rg-dating-resolutions.json which Stage 3 now consults), 0 went against MB. The unresolved 14 (349 records) are mostly ORCHESTRAS — group-type artists never got the +15 birth offset the comparison assumes, plus founding-vs-renaming ambiguity — their corrections currently still flow; owner may want them quarantined. (3) **Live-lexicon 1,126 → 570:** 556 had ZERO corrections at stake (no track datable anyway) — holding them decided nothing, so Stage 3 no longer holds zero-stake cases. The 570 remaining gate 3,633 song corrections (2.2% of 167,708) and bucket as: mixed studio+live boxes 32 (414 songs — genuinely ambiguous per-title: "Intégrale studio & live"), explicit-live titles 235 (1,363 songs — almost certainly live, stays-put default is right), bare-word hits 303 (1,856 songs — the rescuable class: "Wake Up and Live!" is a 1937 SONG TITLE, not a live album; 96 cases with ≥8 songs at stake carry 1,158 of them).
  - **STAGE 2 COMPLETE (Sep 1) — all 15,722 candidate-carrying artists.** Status: 15,321 checked · 343 no-discogs-artist · 58 ambiguous-name (both yield nothing, music stays put). **Identity tiers: id-crosswalk 13,803 · wikidata-hop 499 · name-unique 1,019** — so 93.5% of resolved identity is pure-ID, and the Wikidata hop converted ~a third of what would otherwise have been name-matched. 6,580 artists carry evidence; 32,328 datable records; 15,244 artists carry `titleYears` maps (the per-song foundation).
  - **STAGE 3 FINAL (Sep 1, complete evidence):** 234,234 candidates examined → live untouched **14,539** · not-comp-artist-living **46,834** (re-recording protection) · posthumous-untagged reached **53,440** (rule-2 extension) · inadmissible 505 · song evidence rejected as post-death **36,190**. **167,708 SONG-LEVEL corrections across 8,569 artists.** Album verdicts: **retrospective 82,404** · current 8,761 · no-evidence 58,155. **HELD 1,164** (1,126 live-lexicon, 38 impossible-span, 0 profile-disagreements). **92.6% of retrospective albums cross a decade boundary**; shift from MB year to newest true track: 40+yrs 20,449 · 20–39 30,112 · 10–19 17,512 · 3–9 14,293.
  - **THE FOUNDING CASE, CLOSED END TO END.** Racciatti's "tu corazon" → **1964, via `wikidata-hop`** — the tier added precisely because he carries no direct Discogs relation; without it the reported bug would never have been fixed. Album spans: "Tu corazón" MB 2001 → 1964–1975 · "For Export" MB 2001 → 1964 · "La cumparsita" MB 1999 → 1949–1964. Nina Miranda's "Reliquias" comps (MB 2003/2005) → 1958. Matches the owner's own ℗1963 Sondor evidence to within a year.

Re-run after the sweep finishes for final numbers.
  - **ADDENDUM PASS BUILT — `scripts/build-discogs-profiles.mjs`** (owner-approved: "an independent corroborating fact is worth an hour"). Discogs exposes NO structured location or career-date field (endpoint = name, realname, free-text profile, namevariations, urls), but the profile follows a convention — Racciatti's reads "…(18 October 1918 - 27 May 2000)", matching MB exactly. Extracts life years conservatively (only unambiguous shapes; a wrong extraction would be a FALSE corroboration, worse than none), compares against MB's `cs` minus the birth+15 proxy, ±2 tolerance. name-unique artists only. **MUST NOT run concurrently with the evidence sweep** — same 60 req/min Discogs budget, two at once earns 429s for both. Run after the sweep completes (~1,900 artists, ~35 min).
  - **TRACKLIST INDEX BUILT + PER-SONG JOIN PROVEN (Sep 1).** release.tar.xz verified byte-exact + SHA256; `build-rg-tracklist-index.mjs` scanned 5,736,859 releases → 268,471 kept → **ALL 199,065 unique candidate RGs covered (100%), 0 malformed** (census's 237,414 = candidate ROWS; one RG credited to several artists collapses to 199,065 ids), 149 MB in data/mb-dump/rg-tracklists/. **Prototype join on the ~5,700 artists swept so far: 35,127 comp candidates → 35,094 with tracklists; 982,649 tracks, 204,695 per-song dated (20.8%) from `titleYears` alone; verdicts: 18,938 PURE RETROSPECTIVE (54% — move to the era they represent, as a span), 4,791 mixed/current (stay in release year under the fallback), 11,365 no dates found (stay put).** Real outputs look right: Charly García "12 años" (MB 1994) → true span 1973–1985 (10/18 tracks dated); "Oro" (1994) → 1982–1985; Bonga "Best of" (2016) → 1989–1997. A comp needs only a few dated tracks for a span+verdict, so 20.8% track coverage dates 68% of comps. Prototype: scratchpad persong-join-prototype.mjs — Stage 3 formalizes exactly this.
  - **PER-SONG IS THE DIRECTION (owner go, Aug 31: "Yes, per-song is the direction. Go on the 20.9 GB download.").** release.tar.xz downloading (SHA256 f8a3a795… captured; resumable, --retry-all-errors; ~10h). `scripts/build-rg-tracklist-index.mjs` ready to run when it lands: streams 4.9M releases once, keeps only the 237k candidate RGs' tracklists (raw titles, deduped per RG across releases; recording MBIDs deliberately NOT stored — the per-song join runs on titles against Discogs, and ids that cannot cross that boundary are dead weight). Stage 3's per-song join = tracklist titles × the sweep's persisted `titleYears` maps, all local. The owner's retrospective test becomes computable: all dated tracks older than release year ⇒ the album belongs to the era it represents, AS A SPAN; corrections format must allow year-spans for comps (queue corrections stay title-level — a span never reaches the queue because it plays songs).
  - **MID-SWEEP UPGRADE (2h in, sweep paused → wiped → relaunched; both changes answer the owner's direct questions):** (1) **titleYears persisted per artist** — the full Discogs title→earliest-year map Stage 2 already built in memory and discarded. This IS the per-song foundation: for vintage artists Discogs singles ("A-side / B-side") are per-SONG dates, and the queue's playable unit is a title. Re-deriving it later would cost the whole 16h sweep again; capturing it cost a 2h re-do. (2) **`wikidata-hop` identity tier** between id-crosswalk and name-unique: MB's wikidata rel → the item's P1953 (Discogs id), pure IDs end to end, with a P434 round-trip check (an item naming a DIFFERENT MB artist rejects the hop; plural P1953s = ambiguity = refused). MEASURED: of the 1,919 candidate artists without a usable direct link, **1,456 (75.9%) carry a QID**, and a 16-artist sample found **63% of those carry P1953, with P434 matching on 16/16** — so the hop converts roughly half the would-be name-matched artists to pure-ID identity, exactly the class (incl. non-Latin names) where name matching is least safe. Racciatti — the founding case — resolves through Q10268281 (P434 = his MBID, P1953 = 2364880) without touching his name. Artist index rebuilt with `wd` field (no re-download; the dump was on disk).
  - **END-TO-END PROOF ON THE REPORTED BUG:** id-path alone → no match; + name-search pass → **Discogs 1964, "Tu Corazon / Brasil", label Sondor, country Uruguay — moves back 37 years**, LEAVES the 1990s queue (catalog-tier under move-don't-copy) and arrives IN-ERA in the 1960s. Label+country independently corroborate identity and match the owner's own ℗1963 Sondor evidence to within a year.
  - **Pipeline (origin-corrections pattern — build → arbitrate → committed JSON → read-time apply):** Stage 1 MB RG sweep of the 24k → exact candidate census = **G1 report — DELIVERED**. Stage 2 Discogs-then-iTunes evidence sweep (2-3 + 2-5 nights, $0) = **G2 go on seeing G1 numbers**. Stage 3 local arbitration + held-cases queue for owner (identity bar: romanization + title + year-inside-window + label/format corroboration when present; weak/conflicting = HELD, never moved). Stage 4 apply: rg-date-corrections sharded by artist-mbid prefix, consumed by `rankEraTitles` + artist-era route; per-artist cache invalidation (corrected artists only — no global GATE_PASS storm, YouTube quota stays proportional); era panel shows corrected year with provenance note ("original recordings ~1964 · this edition 2001") = **G4 go**. Nothing built yet — G1 not yet approved.
  - **The case, verified against live MB:** Nina Miranda's ENTIRE MB catalog is 4 release groups dated 2001–2011 (3 tagged Compilation); "Tu corazón" (2001, Compilation) is her nearest dated group to mid-1995, so the queue resolver ranked it `nearby` for the 1990s decade and it played under "▶ Play Uruguay 1996". Racciatti same shape: 8 groups, all dated 1977–2011, 5 comps. MB holds NO original-era release for either artist — the bug is not a wrong query, it is that MB's release-group `first-release-date` = the earliest release MB KNOWS, which for pre-digital catalogs is usually a CD-era reissue, and compilations are legitimately dated at assembly year.
  - **Three year-choosing mechanisms, three exposures:** (1) globe heat (`build-music-data.mjs` → country-year-counts.json) counts raw RELEASE dates — every reissue counts at its own year AND originals double-count; (2) artist-era panel (`/api/explore/artist-era`) uses RG first-release-date — honest mechanism, poisoned input; (3) queue resolver (`/api/queue/artist/[mbid]/[decade]` `rankEraTitles`) era-ranks RG first-release-dates, does NOT read `secondary-types` (the catalog route already parses them — precedent), and the player never surfaces/uses a track's earned `era` label.
  - **Spread (probe of 18 top-weight vintage artists, cs≤1960, across 15 countries, Aug 31):** 862 dated groups → only 29% fall inside the artist's active window; 50% post-date the artist's death/disbandment; Compilation tags catch only ~⅓ of that mass. 5/18 artists (Julio Sosa among them) have ZERO groups dated in their true era — for them the era panel DENIES their era ("nothing on record") while queues serve them into the '80s–'00s. At-risk population: 14,739/137,758 committed roster artists have cs≤1965.
  - **Signals ranked:** (a) `secondary-types` Compilation/Live/Remix — free, definitive, already in the same API response; (b) RG date > artist `end` year (committed in country files) ⇒ not an original-era artifact; (c) gap rule: earliest dated RG ≥ ~25y after `cs` ⇒ the artist's whole MB dating is unusable, fall back to activity window; (d) comp-title lexicon as demotion only (standing lesson 7: a title can't verify kind); (e) TRUE re-dating needs an external source — Discogs masters — MB cannot recover a year it never recorded.
  - **Known traps (stated before building):** Buena Vista class — veterans making genuinely NEW late records (Racciatti led an orchestra to 2000; his "Bien milonguero" 1996 may be a real 1996 record — no life-span rule can tell); genuine posthumous originals; a compilation has NO single true year (recordings span years — "re-date" the artifact = invented precision; the honest era unit for pre-album artists is the activity window); sources disagree on "original" (owner's own evidence: Apple 1963 vs Wikipedia's Sondor LP 1979); classical needs a carve-out (Vivaldi: 85 post-end groups, by three centuries); correction strength will track MB curation quality per country — uneven, must be stated; every cached layer outlives the fix (queue Blobs → GATE_PASS bump exists; panel Blobs v4; 30-day CDN; committed heat JSON).
- **LOCAL MUSICBRAINZ DUMP INDEX — BUILT (Aug 31, 2026; owner-approved: "Go on the dump extract build. Hold Stage 1 until it's validated against the Uruguay shard."):**
  - **WHY NOW, when Aug 29 said "insurance only, low priority".** That scoping asked whether a dump protects against an MB OUTAGE — and the answer was no, caching already does. The owner asked a different question: does it fix THROUGHPUT, given nearly every big job here has been throttle-bound (country precompute 8 passes; gap-fill crosswalk 15,381 entries at 4.53s ≈ 19h over six launches with a whole 420-min window lost to an outage; this census scoped at 15.7h). On that axis it pays for itself on the FIRST sweep.
  - **THE JSON DUMPS, NOT `mbdump.tar.bz2` — a 7× saving found by looking.** The plan was the 6.96 GB core dump plus TSV schema parsing (column order from CreateTables.sql, with the silent-misparse risk that implies). MetaBrainz also publishes per-entity JSON dumps: **release-group.tar.xz is 1 GB** and each line carries `first-release-date`, `primary-type`, `secondary-types` AND `artist-credit[].artist.id` inline — every field the census needs, no schema, no column order, no joins. Verified by a 12 MB range probe before committing to the download (range requests return 206, so it is resumable). artist.tar.xz (2 GB) + area.tar.xz (33 MB) would extend this to the country/origin sweeps later; release.tar.xz is 21 GB and is NOT needed (heat map is out of scope).
  - **TOOLING FACT worth not relearning: `xz` is NOT installed on this Mac.** `bsdtar -J` and Python's `lzma` both are, so nothing needed installing and the no-new-deps constraint held. Node 24.11 also ships `node:sqlite`, but plain sharded JSONL was chosen instead — it matches the country-artists pattern already in the repo.
  - **WHAT WAS BUILT.** `scripts/build-mb-dump-index.mjs` (streams the archive once → `data/mb-dump/rg-by-artist/<xx>.jsonl`, 256 shards keyed on the credited artist's MBID, one row per artist×release-group so split releases are findable from either side; gitignored). `scripts/lib/mbDumpIndex.mjs` (lazy per-shard cache; **returns rows in web-service shape** so consumers need no second code path). `scripts/build-rg-dating-census.mjs --source dump` (default stays `api`, so nothing changes source silently; the dump path has NO page cap, so prolific artists stop being truncated at 300 — a quality upgrade, not just speed). `scripts/validate-mb-dump.mjs` — the gate.
  - **THE GATE PASSED — 182/182 Uruguay artists exact, zero date mismatches, zero type mismatches, zero count gaps.** Snapshot Aug 29 vs live API Aug 31: NO drift at all, which is the number that decides whether a dated snapshot is trustworthy. Download verified byte-exact (1,187,679,180 B) against the published SHA256.
  - **THE GATE CAUGHT TWO REAL DEFECTS IN THE BUILDER — this is why it exists.** (1) **Duplicate rows:** an artist can be credited MORE THAN ONCE on one release group (José Serebrier is composer AND conductor on "Serebrier Conducts Serebrier"), so one row per credit entry reported 76 groups where MB reports 73 — it would have inflated counts and double-counted census candidates. Fixed by deduping artist ids within each release group. (2) **`readline` is WRONG for this data:** it also breaks on a lone `\r`, seeing 4,486,348 lines and choking on 32 "malformed" ones; splitting on `\n` ALONE sees 4,486,319 and parses every single one. Those 32 skips were the tooling silently discarding ~3 real records, not bad data. Do not reintroduce readline here.
  - **PAYOFF, MEASURED: Stage 1 went from 15.7 hours to ~1 minute** (index built 06:53:51, census written 06:55:02), with 0 partial rows where the API path had page caps and 503s.
  - **STATED LIMITS.** Offline tooling only — the live site keeps its cached-API request path untouched. The index is a DATED SNAPSHOT (recorded in index-meta.json and printed by every run): right for historical eras, wrong for anything needing today's data. Does nothing for Discogs (Stage 2 stays 60/min) or YouTube quota.

- **WORK ITEM (PROPOSED, NOT BUILT) — DECADE-AWARE WIDEN (Aug 31, 2026; owner: "sounds right, log it as its own proposal; I'll take it after the dating work"):**
  - **THE GAP IT FILLS.** Today's widen grows the artist POOL by ±5 years but never changes the ERA the resolver picks against: CountryPanel.tsx:626 passes `year={year}` (the original single year) into QueuePlayer, which computes `decade = Math.floor(year/10)*10`. So widening a thin 1996 adds artists who are then asked for 1990s tracks they may equally not have. **Widen adds artists, not eras.** This is pre-existing behaviour, not something the re-dating work introduces — but re-dating makes it matter, because "move, don't copy" deliberately empties the CD-era padding out of decades it never belonged to.
  - **THE SHAPE.** When a place+year queue comes up thin AFTER corrections, offer the surrounding ERA rather than ±5 years of the same one — and SAY SO ("nothing verified for 1996 — play the surrounding era?"). The honesty advantage is the point: it names the thin year instead of quietly refilling it with mislabelled decades, which is the behaviour the owner just ruled out.
  - **MEASURED NEED (Uruguay, all 182 swept artists, worst case).** Across all decades 3–20% of audible artist-slots go quiet (38% only under the unrealistic assumption that corrected records land nowhere). 1960s–80s barely move (1–10%) because corrected records land back INSIDE those decades; the loss concentrates in the 1990s–2020s (19–36%), which is exactly the false padding leaving by design. No Uruguay decade empties under any bound. **The archival-tier 17 are the ones to watch** — a thin country can lose a decade outright; per-country numbers land with the full census.

- **THE SELECTION READS INSTANTLY — CORAL FILL, SELF-BACKGROUNDED PILL, AND THE END OF THE LABEL-CONTRAST BUG CLASS (Aug 31, 2026 — owner-approved at each step; three rounds of owner review before shipping):**
  - **THE BUG CLASS, CLOSED (this is the durable lesson).** Three separate contrast failures shipped in two days and every one was the SAME defect wearing a different face: **a colour computed from a background that varies.** (1) light pin text vanished on hot fills → "fix" flipped it dark over hot bands; (2) the claimed ring's gold vanished on gold ground → "fix" added a dark lens; (3) dark pin text vanished where a country meets the dark sea (owner's screenshot: New Zealand 2019, "New Zealand" half-lost). Each patch only moved the failure to the ground not yet tested. THE CLASS FIX: **stop sampling the background at all.** The selected place's name now rides in a `.pinPill` — a self-backgrounded DOM pill (dark plate, warm-white text, coral border) on the html-marker layer — so its legibility owes nothing to what is underneath, on land, sea, or the seam between them. The heat-sampling label-colour logic and the whole labels-layer pin are DELETED, not patched. **Rule for anything drawn over the choropleth: bring your own background, or be a colour the ramp can never produce. Never compute contrast from the map.**
  - **CORAL IS THE CURSOR'S COLOUR; GOLD IS THE DATA'S (owner-chosen #e4726a).** The old selection was a gold outline that vanished beside a bright neighbour (Australia at 2019 made New Zealand unreadable). Verdigris was tried first, worked functionally, and the owner rejected it as ugly. Coral satisfies the hard constraint — the ember→gold→white-gold ramp cannot produce a pink-red at ANY year, so a selection can never be mistaken for heat — and sits with the brass palette instead of fighting it. Stroke is a lighter tint (`255,176,166`) because a thin line reads darker than a fill. One colour means "you are here" everywhere: countries, US states, UK nations, and Tibet's ring and name.
  - **BORDER-ONLY WAS TRIED, THEN OVERRULED BY THE OWNER — CORRECTLY.** First build kept the heat cap and put coral only on the outline + side walls (+0.04 lift) so a selected country still showed its own data. It failed on bright ground: **Sudan 2019 barely read against its own gold.** Owner overruled their own trade-off: by the time a country is selected the panel is already showing the real data, and the heat is visible before the click and again after closing — losing it for that moment costs nothing. Full coral fill (`228,114,106,0.88`) shipped. Bonus the owner's call bought: heat and selection never occupy the same pixels, so the eye never has to resolve two signals in one shape.
  - **MARKERS COULD PAINT OVER UI — FIXED AT THE LAYER, NOT PER-MARKER.** Owner caught the Tibet ring drawing over the panel's "Filter by genre" pill (South Sudan 2019). Cause: `.scene` was positioned but created NO stacking context, so globe.gl's CSS2D layer — which stamps its own z-index on every marker, ordered by camera distance — competed directly with the panel in the stage's context. Fix: `isolation: isolate` on `.scene` traps those numbers inside the globe, so NO globe marker can ever paint above UI (ring, pill, or anything added later); `.panel` also got an explicit `z-index: 10` so the intent is stated rather than resting on DOM order. Verified by deliberately driving both marker types under the panel — each clips at its edge.
  - **VERIFIED** across every ground that had broken something: Sudan + US 2015 (hottest fills), New Zealand beside white-gold Australia (the reported case), Mongolia 1935 (near-black ground), Tennessee on the region layer, Tibet's ring/name, deep links, and close-clears (pill removed, heat restored). tsc + eslint + production build clean.

- **SELECTION PIN + CLAIMED PLACES ON THE IDLE GLOBE (Aug 30, 2026 — both parts owner-approved via propose-then-build; ring approved after the boundary assessment below):**
  - **THE DASHED RING (shipped).** Claimed places now sit permanently on the idle globe: a dashed ring at the anchor with the place name and contested asterisk, so they are findable by spinning like anywhere else (the owner could not find Tibet by clicking — it existed only via search). Hover gives a card with the approved line and "click for artists"; click flies to the anchor and opens the claimed-place panel; selection lights the ring instead of adding a label pin (naming it twice). Registry-driven off `CLAIMED_PLACES`, so Kurdistan/Sápmi/Azawad-class entries inherit the treatment the day their rosters land. Built on globe.gl's `htmlElementsData` (CSS2D overlay); default occlusion hides it behind the globe and removes its hit testing. **Claimed places are now in the no-WebGL fallback list too** — "findable like anywhere else" holds where there is no globe to spin.
  - **TWO BUGS THE PANE CAUGHT, both worth recording.** (1) **THE STUCK-GLOBE, a mobile-only failure of my first design.** globe.gl raycasts from the CONTAINER in the CAPTURE phase, so a marker click also selects the country underneath and no stopPropagation on the marker can stop it. First fix disabled the globe's pointer interaction on the marker's `pointerenter` and restored it on `pointerleave` — correct for a mouse, fatal on touch: the tap fired enter and never fired leave, leaving `enablePointerInteraction === false`, i.e. **the whole globe permanently unclickable after one tap near Tibet** (verified in the mobile pane). Replaced with a STATELESS check — `onPolygonClick` reads the event's target and ignores clicks that landed on a marker; nothing to release, so nothing can get stuck. The mouse-only hover suppression stays (for the country tooltip surfacing under the marker's own) because mouse leave is reliable, and the mount's `pointerleave` re-arms it regardless. Proven by forcing raycasting ON while clicking the marker — the touch scenario exactly — which opened Tibet, not China. Standing lesson 5's shape: a guard whose release depends on an event that may never fire is not a guard. (2) **GOLD ON GOLD DISAPPEARS.** The marker sits over ground whose heat changes with the year: at 2015 the gold ring and gold name over hot China were effectively invisible. Fixed with a dark lens inside the dashed stroke and a dark plate behind the name — legible on the dark sea and on a white-gold fill. The same class of bug hit the selection pin's label (fixed there by flipping the text near-black over hot bands); **any always-on element over the choropleth needs checking at a HOT year, not just the default one.**
  - **THE STANDING LABEL CAME BACK OFF — EQUAL FOOTING (owner ruling, Aug 31 2026, seen live then reversed; owner-approved before shipping).** Shipped with a permanent name under the ring, and on the live globe that made Tibet **the only named place on the whole map, which reads as emphasis — the opposite of the intent.** Everywhere else you learn a name by hovering. THE RULE NOW: the MARK is permanent, the NAME is not — the ring is Tibet's SHAPE, standing in for the polygon it cannot have, and it earns its name exactly as every other place does.
