Nothing at rest · named on hover · named while selected — which is not a new rule but the one the selection pin established the day before, so Tibet stops being an exception in BOTH directions (not the only named place, and not the only place you cannot name). TWO OBJECTIONS WEIGHED BEFORE BUILDING, both raised by the owner: (a) **phones have no hover** — but countries have no hover on phones either: a country is unnamed until tapped, and the panel names it, so Tibet costs the identical one tap to the identical answer. The genuine mobile risk is not "what is it called" but "is this even tappable", which is a VISIBILITY problem, fixed without text: stripping the label exposed that the label had been carrying the marker (at spin distance over a hot 2015 map the flat ring nearly vanished), so the ring now carries itself — 21→24px, brighter dashes, and a dark halo on BOTH sides of the stroke, verified at hot and cool years, desktop and mobile. (b) **"the text is what stops the ring reading as a city marker" — MY OWN EARLIER ARGUMENT, AND IT WAS WRONG.** Removing the text showed the text was not doing that work: this globe has NO point markers anywhere, so there is no city-pin vocabulary to be misread against, and the form is hollow, dashed and centre-dot-free where pins are solid. RESIDUAL RISK, STATED NOT PAPERED: on a phone someone may never think to tap the ring, and that cannot be tested from here — it needs real use. The dial if it proves too quiet is a ZOOM-GATED name (appears past the state-layer threshold: quiet at a glance, named once attention is committed) — deliberately NOT pre-empted, since it is still special treatment.
  - **A THIRD BUG, AND ONLY PRODUCTION SHOWED IT — THE MARKER MISSED THE SELECTION IT WAS BORN INTO.** On the live site with `?c=tibet` the panel was open for Tibet while the marker sat UNSELECTED and nameless. Cause: globe.gl builds the html-layer element LAZILY, on the layer's first render, which can land AFTER a selection is already applied — a deep link resolves off `onDataSourceChange`, before the first frame. `applySelectionPin` then toggled the class across an empty marker map, and the element created moments later knew nothing about it. Fix: the marker adopts the current selection at construction (`selectedRef` read in `buildClaimedMarker`), so the two orderings converge. LESSON, and it is the reason the production pass exists: **a lazily-built element must adopt current state at birth, not only receive future updates** — every local test had clicked a marker that already existed, so the untested ordering was exactly the one real users hit first via a shared link. Standing lesson 2's shape at render time.
  - **PENDING, OWNER-REQUESTED: EVIDENCE-AS-POINTS (its own proposal, wanted next).** The rejected hull idea has an honest form: on selecting a claimed place, light the DOCUMENTED PLACES themselves as points — Lhasa, Shigatse, Garzê, Dharamsala, New York, Brisbane, Zurich — with no hull, no border, no shape implied. It states what the records actually say (including that most of the catalog was made in exile, which is itself the history) and it cannot be read as a territorial claim. Owner asked for this AFTER the ring; scope, per-artist place sourcing and the exile-vs-origin display question are for that proposal.
  - **THE PIN (shipped).** Every selection path — click, search, Surprise Me, deep link — now pins the selected place on the globe: name label at the centroid (claimed places: at the anchor, asterisk kept) plus the selected polygon holding hover-bright fill and a light stroke for exactly as long as it stays selected. This answers the owner's repeated "Surprise Me spun somewhere and I can't tell where I landed" (Laos et al.). BUILD: GlobeScene gains a `selected` prop (selection lived only in ExploreClient; the globe fired callbacks and forgot) — one effect drives label + highlight off it, replacing the ad-hoc labelsData calls, which also fixes the old wart where Tibet's label lingered after panel close. Label text is warm white but flips near-black over hot-band fills — the selection highlight lerps hot polygons 25% toward white, where light text vanished (caught on the US). Region pins ride the late state-layer load (a region selected from afar pins once features arrive, guarded against selection changes mid-load). Pin re-colors on year/lens changes (heat moves under it). PANE-VERIFIED end-to-end: Laos search (warm-white pin + highlight), US search (dark pin over white-gold), Tibet search ("Tibet *" at Lhasa, panel line intact), Surprise → China 1947 (pin + spotlight), Tennessee search (0.7-size state pin via late layer load), close-clears (labelsData → []), deep link ?y=1969&c=LA cold load. One mid-session `selectedCodeRef is not defined` console error was a stale-HMR closure from the in-flight refactor, not current code (grep-verified, clean after hard reload).
  - **RULING CHANGE (owner, Aug 30): claimed places become ALWAYS-VISIBLE on the idle globe.** The Aug 27 SEARCH-AND-SELECT-ONLY rule is superseded. Owner's stated reasoning, recorded verbatim in spirit: the Aug 27 hiding came from not wanting to make claims about history involving real people and feelings; the position now is simpler — go by what historically happened, where and when, and by what the artists themselves claimed. This is about the music, not the politics around it. NAME-NEVER-DRAW was proposed kept (no neutral Tibet polygon exists to dot — TAR, cholka-sum and 1913–51 are each a side's map); registry-driven treatment so future claimed places inherit it. claimedPlaces.ts doc comment updated to record the change.
  - **BOUNDARY ASSESSMENT (owner-requested third option: draw the ground from our own evidence — where the artists who claimed Tibet actually lived and made music).** MB begin/current areas fetched per-record for all 17 roster artists (15 resolved; Trungpa + Techung 503'd, neither changes the shape — Trungpa's begin resolves only to country, Techung's MB record carries no area at all). FINDING: the evidence cannot produce an honest ground shape, for three separate reasons. (a) RESOLUTION — only 3 artists resolve to actual points inside the claimed region (Lhasa ×2, Shigatse ×1); 5 resolve to "Xizang", the entire TAR — i.e. the PRC's own administrative polygon by another name, because MB's area tree IS an administrative gazetteer: the evidence route lands on somebody's political map anyway; 2 resolve to Garzê Tibetan Autonomous Prefecture (~150k km² of Sichuan, a shape not a point). (b) THE HULL IS AN ARTIFACT — the point-hull is a Lhasa–Shigatse–Garzê sliver in the plateau's southeast, roughly an eighth of its width at globe scale, excluding all of Amdo and western Tibet not because music wasn't made there but because no roster record begins there; it would redraw itself with every roster addition. A border that moves when a database row lands is a statement about MB coverage, not about history. (c) THE HONEST READING LEAVES TIBET — "lived and made music" followed faithfully puts the center of mass in INDIA (Dharamsala, plus NYC/Brisbane/Zurich/Italy): most of the catalog was recorded in exile, so the evidence-shape would draw Tibet south of the Himalaya. RECOMMENDATION: dashed ring at the anchor. **OWNER RULED (Aug 30): ring as specced — the deciding reason was that the evidence is already someone's map. NAME-NEVER-DRAW stands.** Evidence-as-points logged as its own proposal, wanted next (see above).

- **COUNTRY ARTIST PRECOMPUTE — MUSICBRAINZ OUT OF BROWSING (Aug 30, 2026 — owner-approved: cap 2,000, credits list dropped):**
  - **THE OWNER'S INSIGHT, which was the whole build.** The state/UK/historical files never stored per-combo panels — they store ONE compact record per artist and DERIVE any year span and genre lens at read time. Applied to countries: **175 countries, 137,758 artists, 16 MB**, and the 22,225 country×year combinations never materialise. (Estimate was 142,052 / 17.5 MB — within 10%.)
  - **RESULTS.** Panels answer in **6–48 ms from a production build** against 5–20 s live. Egypt: 300-artist pool vs live's 196, with **191 of 195** live artists present and 108 the live path never reached — because the live route only ever fetches ORIGIN_PAGE_SIZE × ORIGIN_PAGES = 200. This is DEEPER than production was, not a degraded copy. Spot-checked and culturally correct: GB/1965 → Beatles, Stones, The Who; NG/1978 → Fela Kuti, Femi Kuti, Tony Allen, Africa 70; BR/1975 → Sérgio Mendes, Os Mutantes, Caetano Veloso, Jobim; JM/1961 → Marley, Scratch Perry, Jimmy Cliff, King Tubby; ZA/1975 → Abdullah Ibrahim, Hugh Masekela (and ZA/2020 → Tyla, so the era derivation genuinely works).
  - **THE ANTARCTICA CREDITS BUG DIED AS A SIDE EFFECT.** Serving origin artists only means there is no releases-derived list left to be wrong: Alanis Morissette, Moby and Major Lazer are gone from Antarctica, whose 2 claimants deepened to 16, and totalCount reads 16 instead of 2,245. The Known Issues entry is closed by this build, not by separate work.
  - **THREE FAILURE-HANDLING BUGS IN THE SWEEP, each found by checking the DATA rather than the exit code — the general lesson.** (1) One page exhausting its retries discarded the whole country: 31 of 175 lost, the US, GB and Brazil among them. (2) Resume OVERWROTE, so a re-sweep that fared worse threw away good rows — **Slovakia went 1,655 → 0**. Now it merges by artist id and recomputes histograms from the union; `complete` is monotonic too. (3) A failed page still STOPPED the country, so later passes re-trod offsets 0..N and died in the same place — the US sat at 1,199 rows across two full passes gaining nothing. **A probe settled it: `country:US` offsets 1500 and 1900 answer fine while 1100 and 1200 return 503, so these were transient refusals, not a deep-paging limit.** Skipping past a refusal (ceiling 6) made every pass reach new ground and the sweep converged 144 → 165 → 171 → 173 → 175.
  - **`truncated` was defined wrong twice.** `claimed > records.length` marked Egypt truncated (its 633-vs-615 gap is per-record rejection, which is the POINT of confirming). `records.length >= CAP` marked Argentina untruncated (claims 9,327, stored 1,986 — the count never reached the cap even though the cap is why we stopped). Correct test is `claimed > CAP`. 50 countries are capped.
  - **DOCUMENTATION CORRECTED RATHER THAN LEFT FLATTERING:** the top-per-decade roster composition is a NO-OP at current settings, because the sweep stops paging AT the cap so there is never a surplus to select from. MusicBrainz's own ordering spreads eras well anyway (Argentina: 1900:7 1930:59 … 2020:16). The pass is kept as a guard for the day fetch depth and cap depth differ; the comment now says so.
  - **READ-TIME, NOT BAKED IN:** origin corrections and genre-family canonicalization both apply at read time, so a corrections or vocabulary rebuild takes effect WITHOUT re-sweeping 175 countries. Without the latter, Aly Bouchnak arrived as four kids-music fragments instead of one "kids music" chip.
  - **SHAPE:** per-country files + a generated index, so a refresh diffs surgically instead of rewriting 16 MB. Served by **dynamic import per country**, so a request parses ~100 KB rather than the whole dataset — verified working in a real production build, which was the technical risk.
  - **NOT REMOVED — MUSICBRAINZ IS STILL IN PLAYING.** The queue resolver calls MB per artist for release groups and channel identity. This removes MB from BROWSING only. The live path also remains for subdivisions and any precompute gap, and the stale-serve fix now mostly covers those rather than countries.
  - **KNOWN LIMITS, stated:** for the 50 capped countries a genre lens searches only the stored 2,000 where the live path queried all of MB; `totalCount` becomes an active-artist count rather than a release count (nothing renders it, so no visible change); new MB artists need a refresh (quarterly, ~26 min at a clean 1.1s pace, `--delay` for a throttled tail).

- **SCOPED BUILD (owner-ruled Aug 29, 2026: cap 2,000, credits list DROPPED; awaiting go) — COUNTRY ARTIST PRECOMPUTE:**
  - **THE INSIGHT (owner's).** The state/UK-nation/historical files never store per-combo panels — they store ONE compact record per artist ({id, name, cs, end, w, t}, 129 B) and derive any year-range + genre-lens panel at read time. 2.9 MB covers 51 regions × 127 years × 20 lenses with zero MB calls. Apply the same pattern to countries.
  - **NUMBERS (measured).** Cap 2,000 per country → 142,052 records, 17.5 MB raw / 6.0 MB gz; **125 of 175 countries stored COMPLETE** (median country has 347 artists). Sweep: ~1,421 MB requests ≈ 26 min. A 2,000-deep pool ranked across the whole roster BEATS the live path, which only ever fetches 200.
  - **PLAN.** (1) `build-country-data.mjs` mirroring build-state-data.mjs, one file per country so refreshes diff surgically. (2) Panel route answers countries from the dataset the way regions already work; live MB stays only as fallback for anything missing. (3) Credits/releases list dropped (owner: "the poisoned layer since day one — losing it is a gain") — this also DELETES the Alanis-in-Antarctica bug as a side effect. (4) totalCount becomes the stored-roster floor, worded honestly like the state path. (5) movedIn/movedOut origin corrections applied at read time, matching current behaviour. (6) Verify EG/JM/PA panels against live, Antarctica shows only its 18 claimants, lens parity. Estimate ~3h wall including the sweep — needs go.
  - Blobs panel cache + stale-serve become the fallback path's insurance rather than the primary; refresh quarterly or on demand.

- **DIAGNOSIS (owner-requested, NOT built) — PLAYBACK'S MUSICBRAINZ DEPENDENCY:**
  - **WHAT THE RESOLVER ACTUALLY NEEDS FROM MB — exactly two calls, per artist, on cache miss only** (route.ts:471-480): `release-group?artist=<mbid>&limit=100` (the era anchor AND the titles multi-track matching runs on) and `artist/<mbid>?inc=url-rels` (the YouTube channel identity anchor). The play-pill chain (lib/play/resolve.ts) needs the same url-rels call at step 1/3; its step 0 is the Blobs queue cache.
  - **TODAY'S FAILURE MODE is already graceful, which bounds the problem:** queue results cache PERMANENTLY in Blobs, and the client skips an artist whose resolve 502s. MB down = every previously-played artist still plays; only first-time resolves contribute nothing until MB returns. The gap is FRESH combos, not playback wholesale. (And YouTube itself is the un-removable dependency — if YT is down nothing plays regardless.)
  - **OPTIONS, honestly:**
    - **(a) MB DUMPS, scoped to the ~142k precomputed artists — the right answer.** Release-group rows (title, date, type: 96 B raw / 18 B gz) and url-rels come from the same CC0 dumps under the SAME MBIDs — no crosswalk. At maybe 3–8 RGs/artist: ~15–40 MB raw. Home: per-artist entries in the existing Blobs store (or R2), read only when live MB fails — no bundle-size problem. What it gets wrong: staleness (new releases absent — nearly irrelevant for historical eras) and it does nothing about YouTube quota/availability; playability checks must stay live (videos get deleted). This CONVERGES with the insurance extract: one artifact, two jobs.
    - **(b) API sweep instead of dumps:** same data, ~284k requests ≈ 3.6 polite days, resumable — zero new tooling, matches the overnight-script culture. Viable but strictly worse than (a) except in familiarity.
    - **(c) DISCOGS — NO for identity, and the owner's own caution is the reason.** Different ID space, so every artist needs the MB↔Discogs crosswalk that produced the 3,907 quarantined links (repair still queued); masters ≠ release groups; and Discogs carries no verified channel rels, so it cannot supply the identity anchor at all. It could substitute era titles only — the half MB dumps already provide without any crosswalk. Verdict: wrong tool here; its place remains the gap-fill dataset it already serves.
    - **(d) PRE-WARMING queues (resolving top artists ahead of visits):** YouTube-quota-bound, ~6 units/artist on the channel path against a 10k/day quota — top-25-per-country ≈ 3 days of quota. Complementary warm-up, not a fallback.
  - **RECOMMENDATION:** (a), as the second phase after the country precompute lands — the 142k stored artists define exactly the universe the resolver will ever try. Low priority stands, per the owner: the permanent Blobs cache already covers the realistic outage.

- **WORK ITEM (SCOPED, NOT BUILT) — MUSICBRAINZ DUMP EXTRACT (Aug 29, 2026):**
  - **WHAT DEPENDS ON MB TODAY.** Survives MB going dark entirely (all committed): the globe and heat map, all 131 artist pages, hits, story cards, the 18k gap-fill dataset, historical areas, US/UK region data, claimed places, stored play links. Degraded but warm: the country panel (Blobs 30d, and now stale-served on failure) and the queue resolver (Blobs permanent). Stops immediately: explore search, artist catalog/album popups, artist-era, artist-links, feed snapshot rebuilds, Discover.
  - **SIZE, measured not guessed.** Trimming 100 REAL artist records and 80 REAL release-group records to exactly the fields Ear Clef reads: an artist row is 132 B raw / ~47 B gzipped; a release-group row 96 B / ~18 B. MB totals (queried, Aug 2026): **2,971,126 artists**, **4,488,303 release groups**, and **1,109,846 artists carrying one of our 175 country codes**.
    - MINIMAL (country-attributed artists + release groups): **~80 MB gzipped** (~550 MB raw).
    - FULL (every artist + every release group): **~210 MB gzipped**.
    - Add the artist→URL relationships the queue resolver needs for channel identity (a small subset of artists have one): single-digit MB.
  - **WHERE IT LIVES WITHOUT A MONTHLY COST.** Not the git repo (GitHub warns at 50 MB, blocks at 100 MB, and it would bloat every clone and Netlify checkout). Not Git LFS (1 GB free bandwidth would go on CI checkouts alone). **Cloudflare R2** is the answer: 10 GB free storage, **zero egress fees**, and the project is already planning an R2 cutover for the Trust in the Sun audio, so the bucket exists anyway — 80 MB is 0.8% of the free tier. Internet Archive is a viable public alternative (MB core data is CC0, so redistribution with attribution is fine).
  - **INSURANCE AND INFRASTRUCTURE ARE DIFFERENT GOALS.** *Insurance* = a cold copy, refreshed quarterly, never read in production; buys the ability to REBUILD if MB shuts down permanently. Cost: a few hours once, then a quarterly refresh, zero monthly. *Infrastructure* = serving from our own copy; needs a query layer over 1.1M artists (country + era + tag), so a database, a sync pipeline, and ownership of both — a system, not a file.
  - **RECOMMENDATION: insurance only.** The request-path problem is already handled by Blobs caching plus the stale-serve fix, so infrastructure would buy little for a lot of ongoing work — against a project constraint that says this is a low-energy-week effort that exists to protect balance, not strain it. State plainly what insurance does and does not do: it does NOT keep the site up during an outage (caching does), it means a permanent MB shutdown costs a rebuild rather than the permanent loss of ever adding a new place or artist.
  - **HONEST COUNTERPOINT worth keeping:** MetaBrainz publishes dumps twice weekly, they are mirrored, and the core data is CC0 — so the realistic risk is losing the API, not losing the data. A documented PROCEDURE plus a periodic extract is close to as good as hoarding one, and the extract's clearest near-term payoff is not insurance at all: it would let the build scripts and sweeps (today's country sweep took ~4 minutes against a 1 req/s courtesy limit) run offline and instantly.

- **IN-PLAYER GENRE CONTROL (Aug 29, 2026 — owner-approved; the thing that was ASKED for, built at last):**
  - **WE HAD BUILT THE WRONG THING, and the owner caught it.** The ask was a control INSIDE the player so a listener can include or exclude genres *while the music is playing*. What existed was the panel's genre chip narrowing the pool at click time. Worse, the existing behaviour was half-reactive and documented as if inert: the comment at CountryPanel says the queue is "deliberately NOT re-keyed on genreFilter", true — but `pool` is a live prop, so changing the chip mid-song silently changed which artists later REFILLS drew from while already-queued tracks stayed. Neither what was asked for nor what the comment claimed.
  - **WHAT SHIPPED.** A collapsible "♪ Genres" control in the queue box listing every genre actually present in THIS queue with counts, each switchable live. Switching one off hides its tracks, skips the playing track if it is one of them, and stops the fill spending requests on those artists. Switching it back on restores them.
  - **GENRES TRAVEL ON THE TRACK, not looked up from the pool** — a widen empties and refills `pool` mid-song, and a queue must not lose the labels its own filter is built on. Same reason the kids-music `demoted` flag is carried per track.
  - **FOUR THINGS THAT ONLY SHOW UP ONCE IT RUNS.** (a) The refill lookahead had to count PLAYABLE tracks ahead, not all tracks, or a queue full of switched-off genres looks deep while playing nothing and never tops up. (b) The exhausted-reset effect had to depend on the exclusions too, since a queue can exhaust purely because everything left was filtered out — switching a genre back on has to reopen it. (c) Row numbers are the position among SHOWN tracks; raw indices read 1, 2, 5, 7. (d) Switching off every genre left audio playing under a panel saying everything was off — it now pauses, remembers why, and resumes from the top when anything is switched back on.
  - **EXCLUSION IS BY ANY TAG, not the leading one:** switching off "metal" means the metal artists, and a band tagged [black metal, rock] is one of them.
  - **VERIFIED IN THE BROWSER on Jamaica 1961:** switching off "dub" while Lee "Scratch" Perry's "Revolution Dub" was playing skipped to Jimmy Cliff and dropped both dub tracks; switching it back on restored them; switching off all 23 genres hid the now-playing row, paused, and showed the restore state; restoring resumed at 1/11.

- **STALE-SERVE ON MUSICBRAINZ FAILURE (Aug 29, 2026 — owner-approved):**
  - **THE BUG, found while assessing the MB dependency rather than from a report.** `readCached` discarded any Blob entry older than the 30-day TTL BEFORE attempting MusicBrainz, and all three failure paths then returned 429/502 without ever looking at what had just been thrown away. A place visited 31 days ago served an error precisely when MusicBrainz was the broken thing. Owner ruling: **throwing away good data at the moment you cannot replace it is the wrong failure.**
  - **THE FIX.** The TTL is now a REFRESH PREFERENCE, not an expiry: a stale entry is held, the live refresh is still attempted first, and every failure path (`RateLimitError`, generic throw, and BOTH non-throwing `!origin` returns) falls back to it. Panel data is history — who recorded in a place in 1961 does not change — so a month-old answer beats an error screen by a wide margin.
  - **THE TRAP THIS WOULD HAVE SHIPPED WITH.** A stale body under the normal `s-maxage=2592000` would PIN a transient outage at the CDN for thirty days, long after MusicBrainz recovered — the outage would outlive its own cause. `respondStale` exists solely to carry `s-maxage=60` with no stale-while-revalidate, so the next request after recovery goes live and self-heals. **When you add a fallback response, check its cache header before anything else.**
  - **HONEST, NOT SILENT.** The payload carries `stored: true` and the panel says "MusicBrainz is unreachable — showing what was stored for this place and era." Stored data is served, never passed off as live.
  - **VERIFIED BY INDUCED FAILURE, not by inspection.** Two temporary probes (a fabricated stale entry and a forced `RateLimitError`) were injected locally, exercised, and reverted. Confirmed: HTTP 200 instead of 429; `cache-control: public, s-maxage=60`; `stored: true`; and the panel rendering the notice above the stored roster. A prior run also confirmed the other direction — with MusicBrainz healthy, a stale entry does NOT short-circuit the live refresh.

- **A PLAYLIST SHOULD ALWAYS PLAY MUSIC — NON-MUSIC VERDICTS (Aug 29, 2026 — owner-approved propose-then-build):**
  - **THREE REPORTED CASES, ALL ON THE ARTIST'S OWN CHANNEL.** Jamaica 1961 opened with `Bob Marley: LEGACY "Punky Reggae Party" | Episode V` (22m documentary, matched because Punky Reggae Party is a real release group); Egypt 2020 with Fatma Said's NDR "Das!" talk-show appearance (3m50s); Panama 2020 with `RUBEN BLADES Y PASIEROS…` (4m9s of him discussing the album, matched on the ALBUM title). Identity was never wrong and every one cleared the duration floor. Standing lesson 7, third instalment.
  - **MEASURED, NOT GUESSED — 170 real queue tracks** from JM 1961, GB 1965, US 1972, BR 1975, EG 2020, NG 1978. The existing NON_SONG_MARKERS hit **zero** of them: the gates were completely blind to this class.
  - **THREE CANDIDATE SIGNALS KILLED BY MEASUREMENT.** (a) `categoryId` — 163/170 are category 10 including every bad one; inverting it flags four legitimate Gilberto Gil live tracks. (b) A duration CEILING — Fela Kuti's real records run 10–30 minutes ("Confusion" 25m37s), so any ceiling catching a 22-minute documentary guts Nigeria, and it would still miss the 3m50s TV appearance. (c) A streaming link in the description — 38% of legitimate tracks have none (the Beatles, King Tubby, Burning Spear) while the Marley documentary has one.
  - **THE UNION THAT SHIPPED.** `television-topic` (YouTube's own knowledge-graph classification — the only signal independent of uploader text) + `title-marker` (widened: episode, epk, press kit, aftershow, listening party, webisode, docuseries) + `describes-talking` (description FIRST LINE only). Excludes **6 of 170**, all verifiably non-music, zero false positives. None is sufficient alone: the topic misses the Blades case, the title misses Fatma Said, the description misses the EPK. Found three the owner had not reported: Peter Gabriel's OVO EPK, his "Full Moon Update" vlog, and a 44-minute Lennon "Listening Party Aftershow".
  - **CALIBRATIONS FORCED BY FALSE POSITIVES, worth keeping.** `part`/`pt.` are NOT title markers — "The Industry - Dark Side Version, Pt.1" is a real track in Egypt's queue. The description test reads the FIRST LINE only (whole-field matching flagged five Elton John tracks whose boilerplate mentions a documentary) and excludes the words documentary/interview/behind-the-scenes (The Who's official promo film for "Who Are You" describes being shot for Jeff Stein's documentary — the music video would have been deleted by its own provenance).
  - **THE RELEASE VALVE WAS APPROVED, BUILT, AND REMOVED.** The design reinstated a television-topic-only verdict when it would leave an artist with nothing, since that signal has a measured false-positive mode (it flags genuine Top of the Pops and Beat-Club performances — but only on third-party REUPLOADS, which the resolver already refuses; 0 false positives across the 170 real candidates). Building it exposed the flaw: **Fatma Said resolves to exactly ONE candidate**, so "reinstate when nothing else survives" put the reported bug straight back. An artist with no music-classified upload now contributes nothing — the cost of a false positive is one artist sitting out a queue of hundreds, never wrong content playing. **A fallback that fires exactly where the bug lives is not a fallback.**
  - **GATE_PASS 2 → 3.** The documentary sits in the Blobs cache under pass 2 and would be served forever otherwise. `snippet` + `topicDetails` ride the videos.list call that already runs — one unit per call whatever parts are asked, so the whole rule costs **zero extra quota**.
  - **RULED, NOT BUILT:** full-album uploads (The Doors' 1h18m "Absolutely Live", Fela LP rips) stay. Still music; people can skip or scrub.

- **FALLBACK COUNTRY LIST — SWEPT, RULED, NO CHANGE (Aug 29, 2026 — owner ruling; NO CODE SHIPPED, deliberately):**
  - **THE ASK.** The non-globe fallback lists every geojson polygon, so it offered Antarctica, the Falkland Islands and the French Southern Lands as places to browse for music. Filter to countries that actually have artists, deciding on the record.
  - **THE OBVIOUS FILTER IS POISONED.** `public/data/country-year-counts.json` is RELEASE counts from a MusicBrainz search query: it claims **7,618 releases for Antarctica and 27,827 for the Falklands**, because digital distributions enumerate every territory on earth. Standing lesson 4, live. Worse, this is not only a list problem — the panel itself uses that layer, so **clicking Antarctica today returns 67 artists including Alanis Morissette**, and the French Southern Lands return Childish Gambino. That is still true and is NOT fixed by this entry.
  - **THE REAL RECORD (scripts/build-country-artist-counts.mjs → data/country-artist-counts.json).** Artists whose OWN MusicBrainz record carries `country: XX`; the search only finds candidates, each is confirmed against its own country field. 175/175 coded features swept.
  - **THE MEASURE THE ASK ASSUMED DOES NOT WORK.** **No country has zero artists** — the lowest is the French Southern Lands with exactly one ("jamuation"). And a threshold cannot separate the intended targets: **Antarctica's 18 outrank Vanuatu (7), Equatorial Guinea (11), Bhutan (14), Western Sahara (15), Oman, Chad and East Timor (17 each)**. Any cut removing Antarctica also removes Aziza Brahim, Mariem Hassan, Jigme Drukpa and Vanessa Quai. These bottom figures are EXACT, not sampled — below the page cap, claimed equals confirmed.
  - **A POPULATION FLOOR WAS PROPOSED AND REJECTED.** The three named territories are precisely the three least-populated features (140 / 2,931 / 4,050) with a 14× gap to Greenland at 57,713, so `POP_EST` would have separated them cleanly. **Owner ruling: no.** The principle is that **the artist's claim wins** — Antarctica's 18 are people who filed themselves there, the panel note already explains the case, and removing them would be the site overruling claims the owner chose to honour. Filter only places with zero artists; none exist; the list is left alone. **The right answer here was to ship nothing.**
  - **SEPARATE, UNRULED, SURFACED NOT ACTED ON:** two geojson features carry no ISO code and are therefore ALREADY invisible in the fallback list — **Northern Cyprus and Somaliland**. Under the claim-wins principle that omission points the opposite way from this ruling, but it is its own decision and was not taken here.

- **OG SHARE CARDS — GENERATED, NOT FILED (Aug 29, 2026 — owner-approved propose-then-build):**
  - **THE ASK, AND WHY ITS ASSUMED FIX DOES NOT WORK.** All 131 artists pointed `meta.ogImage` at `/images/og-<slug>.jpg`; **zero of those files have ever existed** (public/images holds four files total), so every shared link previewed broken. The logged guess was to fall back to the hero image. It cannot work: **130 of 131 heroes are `/images/hero-placeholder.svg`** — a generic grey placeholder, and SVG is not a format Facebook, Slack, X or iMessage will render as a preview. The artwork visitors actually see is built CLIENT-side by HeroArt from a YouTube-thumbnail palette, and no crawler runs it. There was no existing image to fall back TO.
  - **THE FIX.** `app/[slug]/opengraph-image.tsx` — Next's file convention + `ImageResponse`, one real 1200×630 PNG per artist, generated at build time from what the record genuinely holds: name, location, and the one-line `hero.identity` every artist has. Zero files to commit, covers every future artist automatically. Satori constraints noted in the file: flexbox only (no grid), explicit `display` on every container, and the bundled default face rather than Fraunces — next/font/google exposes no binary to hand Satori, and fetching one would put the build on the network.
  - **A DELIBERATE OWNER CHOICE NEARLY DELETED.** The tidy version of this fix clears `meta.ogImage` everywhere and always generates. That would have silently reverted **3d0cd75 — "OG image use Aplete's real hero photo"**: Aplete's entry alone pointed at a file that DOES exist, chosen on purpose. The rule is therefore existence, not pattern: `imageExists()` honours an absolute URL or a path really present in public/, so Aplete keeps his photo and the other 130 fall through to the generated card. **Before clearing a field across a whole dataset, check git for the one row someone set by hand.**
  - **`meta.ogImage` is now an OPT-IN override** and empty on 130 artists — the honest value, since it named files that were never created. Type doc updated to say so.
  - **A SILENT GAP CLOSED ALONG THE WAY.** `twitter.card` was `summary_large_image` with no `twitter:image` at all. The file convention now supplies both, and `generateImageMetadata` gives each card a per-artist alt ("Alanis Morissette on Ear Clef") instead of one generic string across 131 pages.
  - **VERIFIED.** Build generates 300 static pages including every card; PNGs confirmed 1200×630 and inspected by eye (Alanis, Béla Fleck at the roster's longest identity of 148 chars, King Gizzard at the longest name of 32 chars — all fit). Meta tags confirmed in built HTML: generated card for Radiohead, Aplete's real photo preserved, per-artist alt present, zero references to the old broken path. **KNOWN BOUNDARY, stated not assumed:** the roster currently has no non-Latin artist names, so the default face covers it; adding one will need a font with that script or the name will render as tofu.

- **FEED OUTAGE — NULL-MEDIA STORY CARDS (Aug 29, 2026 — live outage, fixed and pushed):**
  - **THE SYMPTOM, AND HOW IT HID.** Logged Aug 10 as "clicking Show 10 more throws an error screen". It was worse: `/feed` was throwing on FIRST LOAD in production, no click needed. Both reports are the same bug — story cards are woven in every 3rd row under a DAILY-SEEDED shuffle, so which day you look decides whether a broken card lands on page 1 or page 2. **A bug whose trigger is seeded by the date will be reported as an interaction bug.** Take the repro as one data point, not the boundary.
  - **ROOT CAUSE.** 77 of 354 cards in lib/stories/cards.json carried `media: null` where the type declares `StoryMediaLink[]`; `card.media.length` in StoryCardView threw, and Next 16's built-in boundary replaced the page with "This page couldn't load". Blast radius was wider than the feed: expanding **Story & Press on 37 artist pages** killed those pages the same way (verified on Alanis Morissette; clean artists verified unaffected on Aphex Twin).
  - **WHERE THE BAD SHAPE CAME FROM.** Not the farm — the /add-artist session pipeline, which hand-authors work files "in the same shape as" scripts/build-story-cards.mjs output. It was not the same shape: `media: null`, `sources[].outlet` instead of `.publisher` (rendering a blank publisher on all 116 of those sources), and card types `context`/`origin`/`award`/`milestone` that the declared union never had. `assemble()` had **no shape check at all** and passed session-authored files straight into the shipped file.
  - **FIXED IN THREE PLACES (standing lesson 2 — fixing a generator does not fix what it already wrote).** (a) StoryCardView guards both arrays at render: a card that cannot render its links costs its links, never the page. (b) `assemble()` now normalizes (`media` → array, `outlet` → `publisher`, malformed entries dropped) and REJECTS unrenderable cards loudly, reporting both counts. (c) cards.json regenerated through it — 77 repaired, 0 rejected, 0 dropped.
  - **THE CHECK THAT MADE THE REPAIR SAFE.** cards.json proved byte-reproducible from the 131 work files before touching anything (0 differing, 0 dropped), which is what made regeneration safe rather than a gamble on hand-edits. Post-repair diff: exactly 77 cards changed, only fields `media` and `sources`, nothing dropped.
  - **A REJECT RULE THAT WOULD HAVE DELETED THE OWNER'S OWN CARDS.** Requiring a citable source looks obviously right and would have dropped 6 cards — all of them `aplete` and `trust-in-the-sun`, first-party entries that legitimately cite nothing external. Sources are NOT required. **Check who a "sensible" validation rule actually removes before shipping it.**
  - **StoryCardType widened** to the eleven kinds actually present. Nothing reads `type` — it is carried, never branched on — which is exactly why four undeclared values shipped without ever failing a check.

- **GENRE FAMILIES + QUEUE OPENING ORDER (Aug 29, 2026 — owner-approved propose-then-build; NOT pushed):**
  - **THE ASK (owner revision of the Egypt 2020 finding).** Kids music stays, but not as a first impression: children's and educational music must not be among the first 5 tracks a visitor hears when they open a country — a general rule, not an Egypt patch — and the class needs a readable genre label listeners can filter by. Explicitly ruled out: a hard-coded demotion list. It works off what the data already says.
  - **WHAT THE DATA SAID.** Aly Bouchnak (EG) is a genuine Egyptian children's artist on his own MB-linked channel — no identity failure and no content-gate failure; the Aug 28 gates passed his uploads correctly (annotation "pet pop dance sing along for toddlers", over the 90s floor). His MB record carries EIGHT children's tags: `kids(1) nursery rhymes(1) children's music(1) kids pop(1) children(0) children's pop(0) lullaby(0) educational(0)`. Exactly ONE reached the pool — `route.ts` drops zero-vote tags, then POOL_TAG_LIMIT=4 cuts the rest. The signal was in the record and the app was throwing it away.
  - **GENRE FAMILIES (lib/explore/genre-families.json + genreFamilies.ts).** Many MB tags → one label. What is hard-coded is VOCABULARY — which words mean a genre — never which artists; an artist joins because their own record says so and leaves the day it changes. Two families, both `demote: true`: **kids music** (10 spellings) and **educational** (language courses, instructional records — kept separate on owner ruling: they are not nursery rhymes). Detection requires at least one family tag with a POSITIVE vote count, so one person's unvoted joke tag cannot reshape a real artist's queue position; Bouchnak clears that bar four times over.
  - **COLLAPSE BEFORE THE CUT, AND PIN THE LABEL.** `canonicalizeWeightedTags` collapses families on the FULL voted list before the top-4 slice, and a demoted label once earned always survives the cut — a label truncated away is a rule that silently stops applying.
  - **NEAR-MISS WORTH RECORDING.** The first version tie-broke equal-vote tags alphabetically. Harmless-looking; it silently changed WHICH of a run of 1-vote tags survived the cut for 13 unrelated Egyptian artists (محمد فؤاد's whole visible tag set changed). Caught only by diffing old vs new output over all 100 artists rather than checking the one artist the fix was for. Now: count descending and nothing else, ties keeping first-appearance order. **A canonicalization pass must be able to prove it changed nothing it was not asked to change.**
  - **QUEUE OPENING ORDER (lib/explore/queueOrder.ts, pure + unit-tested).** LEAD_IN_TRACKS=5, counted in TRACKS HEARD, not pool position — roughly half a pool resolves to no track, so counting artists would enforce something other than the ruling. Two release valves: pool ran dry, and the first-track deadline passing with nothing else found (never report "nothing found" while holding playable tracks). Deferral switches OFF entirely when the pool has no non-demoted artist — one condition covering both "visitor filtered TO kids music" and "the whole place is kids music". Extracted as a pure function precisely so the rule is testable apart from the iframe, worker pool and four abort paths around it.
  - **CACHE BUMP `panel/v3/` → `panel/v4/` (owner ruling).** The top-4 cut is destructive, so a v3 payload has already lost the tags the rule reads; canonicalizing on the way out of the cache would half-apply the rule for 30 days on combos nobody can enumerate. Cost taken knowingly: first visitor to each country+year pays a cold MB fan-out again.
  - **VERIFIED.** 14 unit assertions on the family module + 15 on the ordering rule (one FAILED first and the assertion, not the code, was wrong — 4 lead-in tracks is not 5). Live on dev: Egypt 2020 panel shows ONE chip "kids music · 1 artist" where four fragments would have been, and Bouchnak's track lands at **position 6** with the five ahead of it unrelated. Collateral check across GB/BR/JP/EG (400 artists): exactly 2 pool-tag changes, both genuine children's artists — the second being **Xuxa (BR)**, caught in a country never looked at, which is the general rule working. Owner should know a major Brazilian figure is now held to position 6+ in Brazil queues.
  - **STILL OPEN from the Egypt finding, unruled:** Fatma Said's NDR "DAS!" TV-appearance upload (her own channel, she IS singing) still opens Egypt 2020. That is the missing TV-appearance gate class, NOT this rule's job — and a blanket `live` marker would cost real live records (Crescent's "Live at Weinheim 2022" sits in the same queue).

- **CONTINUOUS QUEUE FILL + WIDEN-YEARS + MULTI-TRACK RESOLVER (Aug 31, 2026 — owner-approved propose-then-build; PUSHED on owner order, commits c33b594/e734515; VERIFIED LIVE — API marker: 6 tracks for one artist, matching dev exactly; production DOM: widen offer renders, and clicking it grew the panel 634 → 773 with the playing track and iframe INTACT):**
  - **DEPLOY-VERIFICATION TRAP WORTH KNOWING (Aug 31, 2026).** The first production DOM check appeared to show the widen bug still live (rows 0, player dead) — but it was a STALE CLIENT BUNDLE cached in the browser, not a production defect: a cache-busted load of the same URL verified correct immediately. Client-rendered features can produce a FALSE NEGATIVE on deploy-verify even after the CDN purge, because the browser holds its own copy of the HTML. Always re-check a failing client-side marker with a cache-busted load before concluding the deploy is broken (the API marker had already verified, which was the clue the two disagreed).
  - **THE ASK.** (a) Keep loading rather than resolving 40 once and stopping; (b) when the year's artists run out, play more songs by artists already played; (c) a widen-year-range button in the queue, same spirit as the panel's sparse-year widen.
  - **CONTINUOUS FILL (QueuePlayer).** One-shot `start()` replaced by a demand-driven engine: INITIAL_BATCH 15, then REFILL_BATCH 10 whenever fewer than REFILL_LOOKAHEAD (10) tracks remain ahead of the playhead. Lookahead beats fixed checkpoints — it self-corrects when a batch resolves nothing (common) or the listener skips hard. QUEUE_MAX is now a 200 sanity ceiling, not a target. Concurrency stays 4 (MB courtesy). Queue starts got CHEAPER: 15 upfront instead of 40, and spend now follows actual listening.
  - **PER-ARTIST DEPTH — the honest cost analysis the owner asked for.** Quota is NOT the limit and neither is MB load: the route already fetches 100 release-groups and (now) 200 uploads per artist, and videos.list batch-checks up to 50 ids for ONE unit. Twelve tracks therefore cost LESS than the old up-to-3 sequential playability checks. What depth actually spends is ERA-TRUTH — candidates rank in-era → nearby → catalog and each track carries the era it earned. The route now returns `tracks[]` (≤ MAX_TRACKS_PER_ARTIST 12) with `track` kept as tracks[0] for compat (lib/play/resolve.ts's queue-cache reader is untouched). UPLOAD_PAGES 2→4: the scan window, not the matching, was the yield limit, and the cost is self-targeting (the scan stops at the last page, so only prolific channels pay 4 units — exactly the artists with depth to give). The SEARCH path is deliberately NOT deepened: 100 units per search, and its identity anchor is a single title (John Mayer rule), so search-sourced artists stay honestly one-song. GATE_PASS bumped to 2.
  - **DEPTH AMPLIFIED THE LESSON-7 EXPOSURE — found and fixed during verification.** Matching a whole catalogue against 200 uploads surfaced what single-picking never did: Amadou & Mariam yielded "Dimanche à Bamako (Documentaire)", "Sabali (Karaoké version)", TV-programme segments, and "Se Te Djon Ye" twice. TWO fixes: (1) NON_SONG_MARKERS gained non-English cognates (documentaire/documental/reportage/entrevista/entretien/bande annonce) plus karaoke — the site is global and so is its junk; karaoke is music but not the artist's record. Coverage is honestly partial, stated not assumed. (2) STRUCTURAL: ONE video per work, choosing the upload whose annotation adds least to the bare title ("(Official Audio)" over "(Eclipse Version)") — depth should mean more of the CATALOGUE, not four uploads of one song. Result on Amadou & Mariam: 11 noisy → 6 distinct real records. Note the era labels legitimately shifted to `nearby`: the gates removed an in-era KARAOKE track, so the queue now prefers a real record from a neighbouring year over a fake one from the right year.
  - **WIDEN BUTTON.** Offered inside the queue box once the pool is exhausted (`⊕ Widen to 1964–1974 for more`), wired to the panel's EXISTING `nearby` state — so list, counts, genre chips and queue widen to one truth. ±5 only (NEARBY_REACH's 11-year window already contains any decade framing). Offered, never performed: it changes what the whole panel claims to show.
  - **TWO REAL BUGS CAUGHT IN VERIFICATION (both would have shipped):** (1) THE SILENT STALL — a batch that resolved nothing changed no state, so the lookahead effect never re-ran and the queue died at 1 track with 40+ artists untried. Fixed with a `fillTick` bumped on every completed batch. (2) THE WIDEN KILLED THE MUSIC — QueuePlayer lives inside `state.status === 'ready'`, and widening reset the panel to 'loading', unmounting the subtree and destroying the player mid-song. Fixed at the source: a refetch over already-shown data now REFRESHES (keeps the ready subtree, shows "Widening to …") instead of reloading; the pool guards were also moved behind `!active` so they decide whether to OFFER a queue and can never tear one down.
  - **VERIFIED (dev, clean reload):** Ghana 1969 → queue fills continuously past its first batch; exhaustion shows ONE coherent end-state line (the old code contradicted itself with "Still digging" beside "Only N verified"); widen grows the panel 634 → 773 artists with the playing track and iframe INTACT and the queue resuming. Amadou & Mariam 2000s returns 6 distinct works; Andrew Bird 2; Ebo Taylor 0 — correctly, he has no MB-linked channel, which is the real reason sparse-place queues are thin (not depth, and not fixable by it — that is the widen and the gap-fill repair).

- **GAP-FILL PULL EXECUTED + STREAMING CLASS SHIPPED TO THE ▶ CHAIN (Aug 30, 2026, same session as the entry below — three owner rulings applied; PUSHED on owner order, commits 3c7aec9/7cda708/0fbe5c6/42b9293; deploy VERIFIED live ~2 min post-push by capped positive-marker check — Nawang Khechog serves spotify play, T.O. Jazz serves play:null + Discogs read — so the CDN purge has cleared the wrong-artist pills from production):**
  - **RULING 1 APPLIED — THE PULL.** Owner: wrong-artist ▶ is the same class as the phishing link (the site promising what it can't deliver); pull now, repair later, scope delegated. SCOPE CHOSEN: quarantined every committed YouTube link with NO identity evidence — kept only the 2,922 whose upload title names the artist (the audit's reliable-positive bar, 11–12/12 sample, stacked on release-attachment). Quarantined 3,907: 3,849 uncorroborated + 57 unmapped keys + 1 gone. The 397 Internet Archive entries stand (exact-alias creator match — different, unbroken method); swept nulls stand. MECHANISM: `identityUnverified: true` per entry (`scripts/pull-unverified-extra-play.mjs`, re-runnable); `committedExtraPlay()` answers `undefined` for quarantined keys, so the live IA chain may still verify honestly — the stored URL is PRESERVED for repair, quarantine is an accessor verdict, not data destruction. The accessor is the single choke point: committed verdicts serve BEFORE Blobs, so no Blobs poison exists for this class; the only stale copies are 30-day CDN edge entries, which the next deploy's purge clears. VERIFIED: T.O. Jazz (dg:1549475) now returns play:null with the Discogs read link intact.
  - **REPAIR — OWN WORK ITEM, NOT STARTED (owner: "fixed properly, not fast").** Goal: re-verify all 6,829 links under the full John Mayer bar — corroborate the upload title against the artist's OWN release/track titles (Discogs release titles are already in the dataset's sourcing), not bare name-in-title. That bar should be applied to BOTH buckets: the 2,922 kept links passed a weaker bar and deserve the stronger one; the 3,849 quarantined include correct-but-unmatchable entries (song-title-only uploads, cross-script names like 陳寶珠 = Connie Chan Po Chu) that per-artist evidence can rescue. Inputs ready: titles + durations committed, `data/gap-fill-play-identity-audit.json` triage, root cause documented in `scripts/audit-extra-play-identity.mjs`. Design belongs to its own session; propose-before-build applies.
  - **RULINGS 2+3 APPLIED — PLATFORM STREAMING JOINS THE ▶ CHAIN, FREE BEFORE PAID.** New PlayKinds `spotify | deezer | apple-music | tidal | amazon-music` (host-classified in `classifyRelations` — "types vary; hostnames don't lie"; itunes.apple.com deliberately excluded: 'purchase for download' is a shop, not a play). Chain order: queue cache → platform rels → IA → STREAMING → null; streaming is LAST because everything above plays free and direct. Free-before-paid (owner: paid is a fallback, not a peer): the pick is always `streaming[0]` from a free-first ordering; FREE_STREAMING_KINDS = {spotify, deezer} in `lib/play/types.ts` — a fact about the services, not MB's rel typing. SAFETY: platform hosts cannot be domain-squatted; the Aug-29 phishing class structurally cannot recur here.
  - **LISTEN-SERVICE PREFERENCE STAYS OUT OF THE CACHE.** `ArtistPlay.streaming` ships the full free-first list ONLY when play resolved to the streaming fallback; `lib/play/pick.ts` swaps in the fan's chosen service at render time (spotify/appleMusic/amazonMusic map to kinds; youtube maps to nothing — that fan wants the verified-video chain, which already ran). A verified video/Bandcamp/archive play is NEVER downgraded to an app page. The 30-day Blobs+CDN cache stays shared, never keyed by service. 8-case unit run on the compiled module: all pass.
  - **CACHE (lesson 2, surgical):** pre-streaming cached mb: NULLS re-resolve (`RESOLVER_PASS` marker in CachedPlay, `preStreamingNull` check); every non-null cached result outranks streaming and stands; post-pass nulls stand their 30 days; non-mb nulls gain nothing from streaming and stand.
  - **VERIFIED LIVE (dev):** Nawang Khechog — the artist whose phishing incident pulled officials — resolves `{play: spotify, streaming:[spotify]}`, his ▶ honestly back. E.T. Mensah (Spotify + Tidal rels, no ▶ before): play=spotify, tidal behind it in the list; renders "Listen on Spotify" ▶ in the Ghana panel DOM, where five streaming badges now show in the visible tier. Guy Warren proves no-downgrade: IA item wins, no streaming list shipped. Survey basis for the class (36 explore-pool artists): 3 had a ▶, 8 more gain one — roughly 4× coverage, zero extra requests (same url-rels call).

- **QUEUE FEATURES 1 & 3 BUILT; FEATURE 2 BLOCKED ON A DATA DEFECT IT UNCOVERED (Aug 30, 2026 — owner-approved propose-then-build; NOT pushed; the defect and pull question RULED — see the entry above):**
  - **(1) Lesson-7 content gates in the queue resolver — BUILT, VERIFIED.** New `lib/play/contentGates.ts` (MIN_DURATION_SECONDS 90, NON_SONG_MARKERS, annotation-scoped `annotationOf`) ported from the hits sweep and imported by `app/api/queue/artist/[mbid]/[decade]/route.ts`. Annotation gate runs on BOTH candidate sources (channel uploads and corroborated search hits) before any YouTube spend — free rejection; duration floor rides the existing 1-unit `videos.list` (now `part=status,contentDetails`). Closes the Aug-10 Egypt-2020 class (AI kids' video + TV interview as tracks 1–2). Cache: `GATE_PASS = 1` written on every new entry; entries without it are treated as MISSES and re-resolve in full (owner ruling — a duration-only recheck cannot see the interview class, standing lesson 2). Tracks now also store `eraTitle` so a future rules change can re-gate cached entries without re-deriving the era pick. Verified by compiling the real module and exercising 20 cases (Madonna trailer, behind-the-scenes, interview, 9s Short, 25s clip, the 90s boundary, 11-minute video, non-Latin titles, empty inputs): all pass, including the marker-inside-the-work-title cases annotation scoping exists to protect ("Live and Let Die", "Interview" by Nirvana).
  - **(3) Genre-aware queues — BUILT, VERIFIED IN THE DOM.** CountryPanel now passes the genre-FILTERED pool to QueuePlayer and labels the button "▶ Play {genre} — {place} {year}". Deliberately NOT re-keyed on genreFilter: the pool is read at click time, so changing the filter mid-song leaves the playing queue standing instead of tearing down the player. Verified live on Ghana 1969 + highlife: button label changes, queue resolves Gyedu-Blay Ambolley — "Simigwa-Do" (a real Ghanaian highlife record) and says "Only 1 verified track here — honest, not padded."
  - **(2) Gap-fill queues via pre-verified videos — BUILT BUT GATED OFF (`GAP_FILL_QUEUES_ENABLED = false` in CountryPanel).** Server plumbing is done and correct: `extraQueueTrack()` in `lib/explore/extraPlay.ts`, `queueTrack` shaped per entry in `extraArtistsServer.ts`, consumed by a no-request instant path in QueuePlayer. Payload carries ONLY `{videoId, title}` per entry — verified the dataset does not ship (no `durationSeconds`/`queueEligible` in the response; Aug-17 bandwidth lesson holds). It WORKS: Ghana 1969 filled 8 tracks in 3 seconds. It is off because of what those 8 tracks turned out to be — see below.
  - **THE DEFECT — GAP-FILL PLAY LINKS FREQUENTLY NAME THE WRONG ARTIST (live in production on the pills, NOT introduced by this session).** The owner-approved enrichment sweep (`scripts/enrich-extra-play.mjs`, 6,828 entries, 137 quota units) added upload titles + durations to `lib/explore/extra-play.json` — and the titles made a pre-existing defect legible for the first time. "T.O. Jazz" (Ghana) plays a Leipzig boys' choir Christmas carol; "C.K. Mann & His Carousel 7" plays Keith Jarrett's Köln Concert; "Louis Armstrong And His All-Stars" plays an Exium techno remix; "Fariala Yembo" plays Jane's Addiction. VERIFIED PRE-EXISTING: the stored URLs are byte-identical before and after enrichment (checked against a backup) — the sweep only fetched titles. These are the ▶ pills shipping TODAY.
    - **ROOT CAUSE** (`scripts/build-extra-play.mjs`, `discogsReleaseVideoIds`): it collects every community-submitted video attached to a Discogs release the artist appears on, breaks at the first release carrying any video, and takes the first PLAYABLE id. On a compilation or various-artists release those videos are OTHER artists' tracks, and playability says nothing about whose record it is. Standing lesson 1 exactly — the release was verified against the artist, and the halo was extended to videos merely attached to it. It is also lesson 7 one level up: the sweep verified WHAT the artifact is (a playable video) and never WHO it is.
    - **SCALE (triage, not a verdict — `scripts/audit-extra-play-identity.mjs`, rows in `data/gap-fill-play-identity-audit.json`):** of 6,771 titled YouTube links, 2,922 (43.2%) have a title naming the artist and 3,849 (56.8%) do not. Neither number is an error count. A 12-row sample of the corroborated bucket read as 11–12 correct, so name-in-title is a RELIABLE POSITIVE. A 25-row sample of the uncorroborated bucket read as roughly two-thirds clearly wrong, the rest either correct-with-song-title-only or correct-but-unmatchable across scripts (陳寶珠 IS Connie Chan Po Chu). Order-of-magnitude estimate: **~2,000–2,700 wrong links, ~30–40% of the titled corpus.** No automated rule separates the uncorroborated bucket — that is judgment on evidence, per artist or per class, and it is the owner's.
    - **WHY GATED RATHER THAN SHIPPED:** a wrong pill is one bad click the visitor can judge; a wrong queue entry AUTO-PLAYS, and the queue would have multiplied ~2,500 misattributions into unattended playback. Feature 2 is one line from live once the dataset is ruled on.
    - **AWAITING OWNER RULING:** whether to repair (re-sweep with an identity bar — title corroboration against the artist's own release/track titles, the John Mayer rule applied to gap-fill), to demote the uncorroborated class to read-only pills, or to pull gap-fill ▶ pending repair. The pills are live now, which makes this time-sensitive but NOT a safety issue (no phishing class here — these are ordinary music videos by the wrong artist).

- **PHISHING INCIDENT — OFFICIAL-SITE LINKS PULLED FROM EVERY CHAIN (Aug 29, 2026, URGENT user-safety; mitigation SHIPPED AND PUSHED same hour, commit 2794680):** Nawang Khechog's ▶ on the Tibet panel opened a Chrome-flagged phishing page — MB's official-homepage rel (nawangkhechog.com) is an EXPIRED DOMAIN repurchased maliciously, now 301ing into the bwo99/girinesia gambling-phishing chain. ROOT CAUSE: isParkedOrDead() answers "is it alive?", not "is it safe?" — a live malicious site passes every parked/dead marker; this is the Aug-10 flag (Mohamed Fouad WebSite.ws lander) realized as an active threat, and there is NO oracle that distinguishes a live official site from a live phishing site. RULING APPLIED (class, not case): the official-site kind earns no ▶, ever — pulled from lib/play/resolve.ts order; 'official homepage' no longer served by /api/explore/artist-links; links.website dropped from the pill smart-chain (falls to Wikipedia→MB). CACHED POISON KILLED: play Blobs key bumped to v2: (30-day entries die) + the deploy's CDN purge clears the 30-day edge cache; browser-session caches die on reload. EXPOSURE CLASS (now empty): any mb: artist with an official-homepage rel and no YT/Bandcamp/SoundCloud rel — enumeration of Blobs impossible, class elimination chosen over enumeration. UNAFFECTED: hits-play (YouTube-verified only), archival links (owner allowlist), gap-fill pills (Discogs/WD pages), queue tracks (YouTube). Officials may return ONLY behind a future owner-ruled allowlist. VERIFIED: Nawang Khechog resolves play:null + MB read link locally; capped production check armed. **QUEUE-HANG DIAGNOSIS (bug 2, report only — fix needs go):** QueuePlayer's fill loop is STRICTLY SEQUENTIAL over up to QUEUE_MAX=40 artists, each await fetch carries the abort signal but NO TIMEOUT and there is NO overall deadline; "Finding the sound of…" shows until the FIRST track resolves. A pool whose early artists are all cache-misses resolving to track:null (sparse countries — each server resolution: 2 MB calls with 3×8s retry envelopes + pacing + YT channel/search calls ≈ 10-30s typical, worse on bad nights) = 40 × 15-20s sequential = the reported 10+ minutes, all "working as coded". FIX SHIPPED (Aug 29, owner go): QueuePlayer fill is now a BOUNDED WORKER POOL — QUEUE_CONCURRENCY 4 (kept small for MB courtesy on cache-miss bursts), per-request AbortSignal.any(controller, timeout 20s) so one hung resolve skips ONE artist, FIRST_TRACK_DEADLINE 60s with no track → controller.abort + honest sparse-voice message ("Couldn't find the sound of X tonight — the pills below still play one by one"), quota-503 aborts the pool cleanly, unmount aborts leave state alone. Workers pull pool-ranked indices in order; first resolved track plays whichever worker lands it. Verified: MD 2015 queue → player mounted + first track inside ~12s with "Still digging — 1 track so far…"; pool-logic unit check (no dupes/skips).

- **PRESENCE MODEL — THE GOVERNING PRINCIPLE REPLACED + PHASE 0/1 SHIPPED (Aug 25, 2026, owner-designed and owner-approved):** the locked editorial line "only artists FROM a place appear — distribution reach is not local culture" is RETIRED. The principle is now **WHERE THE MUSIC HAPPENED** — based, emerged, performed/recorded — never birthplace alone and never mere distribution. An artist is EDGES (artist × place × era × kind × evidence), era-gated by the slider; one artist may hold several. **EDGE KINDS + EVIDENCE BARS:** based (lived+worked: MB area + career docs / wd P551/P937 / owner; counts) · emerged (career BEGAN there: begin-area/formation ONLY with career-start evidence — birth alone is never an edge, THE ASUNCIÓN TEST, owner-confirmed on their own case: born Asunción, left at 2, career Las Vegas → LV only, PY is a biographical note; birthplace notes exist ONLY for hand-added roster artists, never the 18k; counts) · performed/recorded (RARE; requires an OWNER-EDITED ALLOWLIST source or per-case owner ruling — without the gate every tour stop becomes presence and the fame leak reopens through the performed door; counts) · **archive** (identity established, origin affirmatively unestablished; record-verified pressing country per lesson 4 is the ENTIRE claim; NEVER counts) · distributed-only (not presence; exclusions unchanged). **COUNTING IS PRESENCES-NOT-PERSONS (owner-approved):** an artist genuinely active in two places in one year counts in both. **ABOUTNESS ≠ PRESENCE:** music about/for a place (Khalifé×PS) is a LOGGED FUTURE FEATURE ("songs written about this place" / Artist Chapters), never an edge. **PHASE 0 SHIPPED:** scripts/apply-presence-rulings.mjs (--from-rerun-report / --case CC|dg|ID --presence archive|full for owner vetoes); 45 entries (worksheet D 42 id-confirmed + E 3 id-proven-other — origin-unknown by construction) now carry presence:"archive" in lib/explore/extra-artists.json; verdicts + overrides record archive-keep (vetoable per case); dataset total unchanged 18,667 — NOTHING deleted. The 2,174 uncorroborated-kept + presumed-locals are UNTOUCHED (their presumption is the approved gap-fill design; never mass-demote). **PHASE 1 SHIPPED:** extraArtistGroupsFor returns {dated, undated, archive} (archive era-gated the same way — pressing years are real dates; pre-change cached payloads degrade to no section); CountryPanel renders the ARCHIVE DIVIDER after the artist list, gated !genre: heading "From the {Country} record archive", copy (owner-approved voice, plain, no apology): **"These artists appear on records pressed here — that much is verified. Where they were from, no database says. We'd rather play the music than pretend to know."** Archive entries are OUT of the pool, the header count, the genre filter, rankings, QueuePlayer (MB-only already) and the heat map (MB-only already); same pills, verified-play ▶ badges work (playKey chain is id-based, origin-independent — verified live: MD panel archive pills resolve YouTube). VERIFIED: tsc clean; /api/explore/MD/2010-2023 serves 8 archive entries, GM 1, era gate correct at y=2015 (5 of 8 in span); panel section rendered + screenshot-checked. WORKSHEET UPDATED: D/E sections now show "archive · applied — veto or upgrade per case"; the open judgment queue is effectively A4+B9+C6+F44. **PHASES PENDING (each its own proposal+go):** 2 = convert the ~72 keep-with-note metropole/diaspora corpus into dated second edges (evidence already in the notes); 3 = MB-layer semantics (area/begin-area as edges, panel copy, heatmap rebuild under presences-not-persons); 4 = wd P937/P551 dated-edge sweep. The origin-corrections layer needs a semantic audit in phase 3 (some Aug-8 corrections invert under the new principle). **OWNER RULING (same day): worksheet A1–A4 APPROVED and applied** — 5 cross-pool-consistency exclusions (TT The Fifth Dimension dg191325, SR David Rose dg311166, JO+KW Amr Diab dg99728, PS سعدون جابر dg3139999), dataset 18,667→18,662, verdicts flipped to owner-ruling-cross-pool-consistency, overrides recorded; the TT Fifth Dimension keep-note from the re-run is superseded (crosswalk leg outranks reverse leg — the page itself is the US group's).
