Five held cases were never even exact name matches (hitBasis "name" via MB alias search): PG "Good Guys Vibration II"→Good Guys, NI "Guayo González"→**Eduardo Gonzalez**, TT "Fortunia Ruiz"→**Louis Ruiz**, TT "Frank Francis"→Frankie Francis, TT "The Esquires Combo"→The Esquires. **DECISION TREE v2** (scripts/lib/held-rerun-tree.mjs): same ruling — the discriminator is ORIGIN, never area, never fame — but identity is now EVIDENCE, ranked id-proven / id-disproven / name-only, and id evidence is tested BEFORE origin so a disproven collision keeps regardless of what the foreign artist's Wikidata says (BS Sweet Exorcist keeps despite wd "British music duo"; HN Julio Ramírez keeps despite wd Mexican). MB area is still never read as origin (Naghma stands). **OUTCOMES: 59 keeps APPLIED** (53 id-disproven + 6 id-proven-local: BD H2SO4→Bangladesh, LK Forlorn Hope→Sri Lanka, NI The Bad Boys→Nicaragua, VN Thanh Tâm→Nguyễn Thị Thanh Tâm, GH Bola→Ghana, TT King Radio→Trinidad — MB knew the local act and the dedup matched a foreign homonym); note-only writes, dataset total UNCHANGED at 18,676, zero removals. **18 exclusions PROPOSED, not applied** (9 id-confirmed incl. HN Los Caminantes wd Q96, JO يارا, IQ ایرج, KW رباب/هيام يونس/عبد الرب إدريس/محمد محي, GH The Funkees→Nigeria + Greyhound wd Q145; 9 name-only and explicitly flagged as resting on the leg this pass measured 33% wrong). **95 residue for the owner** — 6 reserved history classes (4 now id-CONFIRMED: TL Tonny Pereira→MB Indonesia, NP Tibetan band→MB India, XK Silent Existence + Alavux→MB Serbia; TL Jerry Btn and KP 김승연 have no id evidence), 42 id-confirmed-but-origin-unknown, 44 name-only, 3 id-proven-other; 43 carry verbatim Discogs profile text attached as judgment material. **⚠ THE MIRROR CHECK — THE POINT OF THE SESSION:** the crosswalk reads the DATASET, so the 104 entries already EXCLUDED by owner rulings had never been tested by it. All 104 were crosswalked by Discogs-page URL: **78 CONFIRMED at id level, 3 different-identity, 23 with no MB link, and ZERO pool-local matches — no wrong exclusion found.** Zarsanga (the Aug-10 "Beatles pattern at regional scale" ruling) is id-confirmed Pakistani; Fundi Konde/Safari Sound Band Kenyan; Carlos Malcolm US; Verónica Castro Mexican. ONE BASIS CORRECTION: **AL Capital T dg:59661 has the right verdict for the wrong reason** — MB links that page to "Capital T, UK garage producer Trevor Davy", NOT the Kosovar rapper, so the recorded basis "Kosovo-origin → belongs to XK's pool" is wrong and must not be used to add him to XK. RESIDUAL RISK STATED: the 23 no-MB-link exclusions still rest on the Aug-11 name match alone and are untestable by this method. **CROSS-POOL SWEEP: 4 genuine holes** (dg:99728 Amr Diab live in JO+KW, dg:3139999 سعدون جابر live in PS, dg:191325 The Fifth Dimension live in TT, dg:311166 David Rose live in SR — each id-confirmed by the crosswalk in the live pool); dg:3016481 حميد الشاعري excluded from PS+KW but kept in LY is correctly CONSISTENT (LY is his origin, Q1016) — the sweep now marks that rather than flagging it. **OWNER RULINGS (Aug 25, same session, applied):** the **9 id-proven exclusions APPROVED and applied** (HN Los Caminantes, JO يارا, IQ ایرج, KW هيام يونس/رباب/محمد محي/عبد الرب إدريس, GH The Funkees/Greyhound — dataset 18,676→18,667; verdicts flipped durably to owner-ruling-held-rerun-id-proven; overrides updated so re-gathers hold, replacing the old held pins on Greyhound and Los Caminantes); the **9 name-only proposals HELD by owner ruling** — that identity leg just measured 33% wrong, so they join the worksheet with the residue instead of being acted on; **Capital T basis CORRECTED** in work verdicts + overrides (AL|dg|59661: exclusion stands, but the page is MB-id-linked to the UK garage producer Trevor Davy, not the Kosovar rapper — never seed XK from this Discogs id; confirmed on both legs: MB URL rel + Discogs realname "Trevor Davy"); the 4 cross-pool holes NOT applied — they carry recommendations in the worksheet and await the owner's explicit go. RULING WORKSHEET: the 95 residue + 9 name-only + 4 consistency items published as a private Artifact (owner link in chat); evidence per case at a glance, stable case codes for chat rulings.

### #1 HITS SECTION + UK NATIONS (Aug 20, 2026 — both owner-approved via propose-then-build)

- **#1 HITS PANEL SECTION (Feature 1, US+UK Phase 1):** "This year's #1 hits in [place]" on the US/GB COUNTRY panels only, strict-sourcing gate (Gate v3 Tier 1): section exists ONLY where an authoritative chart covers the place+era — Billboard Hot 100 (Aug 1958→, pre-1958 Best Sellers ruled OUT) and OCC Official Singles Chart (Nov 1952→, OCC canon: NME 1952–60, Record Retailer 1960–69). Everywhere else /api/hits 404s and nothing renders. SOURCING RULING: authority is the chart; transport is Wikipedia's number-ones list pages via the sanctioned MediaWiki API (owner-approved Aug 20 — billboard.com ToS prohibits scraping and serves automation a TollBit AI paywall; officialcharts.com copyright notice prohibits bulk reproduction + UK database right; both sites stay fine for manual browser spot-checks). INGEST: scripts/build-number-ones.mjs (resumable page cache data/wiki-cache/, gitignored) → lib/hits/number-ones-{us,uk}.json (committed, ~260KB total; US 1,391 entries 1958–2026 — weekly issue rows grouped by reign, weeks = weeks-at-#1 WITHIN the year so Dec-spanning reigns appear in both years; UK 1,543 reigns 1952–2026 from decade pages, assigned to reached-#1 year, weeks = full-reign total). Parser is header-driven with rowspan-grid expansion; loud validation (US yearly week-sums 48–56, 1958 partial-year 20–24; UK every year ≥1 reign; zero-entry pages throw). Verified subtleties: "Old Town Road" splits solo/remix credit reigns; "She Loves You" carries both its separate 1963 reigns; Al Martino 1952-11-14 opens the UK file. SERVING: lib/hits/hits.ts (SERVER-ONLY imports, bundle-safe) → /api/hits/[country]/[span] (30d CDN cache; span-merge folds duplicate title+artist summing weeks; ranked weeks-desc then chronological; ENTRY_CAP 105 with honest capped note). UI: components/explore/HitsSection.tsx below the artists list, above WhatWasPlaying — top 5, "Show 20 more ↓" reveals, attribution line ("Source: Billboard Hot 100 (compiled via Wikipedia)" / OCC equivalent, owner-ruled wording). PLAY: locked play-chain policy applies — per-hit ▶ ONLY when lib/hits/hits-play.json (join key `chart|artist|title`, attached at RESPONSE time) carries a playability-checked videoId; "▶ Play all" renders a pre-resolved queue through QueuePlayer's new `preresolved` mode (skips the resolver walk, plays instantly). hits-play.json is EMPTY until the video verification sweep runs — owner gives that its OWN go (est. 3–6 nights of free YouTube quota; exact-normalized title match from the chart entry, official/Topic channels only, honest null otherwise). SPOT-CHECK PROTOCOL (owner-mandated pre-ship, COMPLETE Aug 20): UK 6/6 VERIFIED by Claude against OCC's own archive in-browser (random 1981/2006/2015/2023/2024 + 1952 first-chart bonus: Here in My Heart / Al Martino, 14 Nov 1952; note OCC re-dated historical weeks so its week-endings sit ±1 day from Wikipedia's convention — same weeks, same facts). US 5/5 VERIFIED BY THE OWNER in their own browser (billboard.com serves automated browsers a TollBit AI paywall; NOT bypassed — owner performed the checks per protocol): 1972-04-15 "The First Time Ever I Saw Your Face" Roberta Flack · 1974-03-02 "Seasons in the Sun" Terry Jacks · 1999-10-23 "Smooth" Santana feat. Rob Thomas · 2023-05-06 "Last Night" Morgan Wallen · 2024-07-27 "A Bar Song (Tipsy)" Shaboozey — all matched exactly. Both charts cleared to ship.
- **HITS VERIFIED-PLAY SWEEP (owner GO, Aug 20, 2026 — nightly windows, one push per night):** scripts/build-hits-play.mjs, resumable via gitignored data/hits-play-work.json; outputs lib/hits/hits-play.json (playKey → videoId) + lib/hits/hits-artists.json (credit → roster slug) + data/hits-play-report.json. CHAIN, per credited artist (grouped so one channel scan serves all that artist's hits): (1) IDENTITY — roster exact-name → its committed MBID, else MB artist search accepting ONLY exact-normalized name equality (≤4 candidates); (2) CORROBORATION (the John Mayer rule) — a candidate is CONFIRMED only when the chart title appears in that MBID's OWN release groups; TWO candidates corroborating the same title = ambiguity → null, never a guess (logged to report.ambiguousIdentities); (3) CHANNEL — roster's committed channelId, else the confirmed MBID's MB url-rel; (4) exact-normalized title match over ≤10 upload pages (500 videos), decorations stripped ("Artist - ", trailing "(Official Video)"/"[…]") but what remains must match EXACTLY; (5) videos.list playability (public + embeddable). NO YouTube search fallback, no fuzzy, per owner. Sweep order is a deterministic name-hash SHUFFLE, not chart order — the corpus spans 1952–2026 and channel coverage varies hugely by era, so a chronological sweep would make each night's match rate a statement about one decade; the hash keeps nightly samples representative and resumes reproducible. ARTIST LINKS (owner addition): hit rows link to a roster page ONLY on confirmed-MBID equality with the roster — identity-grade, never name-grade; joint credits resolve to no single artist and stay unlinked BY CONSTRUCTION (no name parsing decides it). Gap-fill candidates are REPORTED for owner review (exact name + era overlap), never auto-linked — no MBID exists to confirm them. **CHANNEL RULINGS (owner, Aug 21, 2026 — two rulings, both encoded):** (1) CHANNEL-SCOPED SEARCH APPROVED WITH A HARD BOUNDARY: searching WITHIN an already-verified channelId is fine — identity is guaranteed by the channelId parameter, so a wrong artist is structurally impossible, categorically unlike the John Mayer failure which derived identity FROM the search. The approval covers SEARCHING a known channel; it does NOT cover DISCOVERING a channel by name — `"<Artist> - Topic"` as a query is "name matching wearing a costume" and stays BANNED. Implemented as a budget-ordered phase (`--phase search`, 100 units/query, queue persisted in the work file) that runs only after the cheap pass. (2) TOPIC CHANNELS ARE A LEGITIMATE VERIFIED TIER, SITE-WIDE — auto-generated from official distribution, so content is official by construction; ranked below the artist's own channel, above nothing. This also SETTLES the open /add-artist question (Topic videos had been rejected for Neutral Milk Hotel); .claude/commands/add-artist.md updated to the tier order own → label/estate → Topic, with the same evidence-not-name boundary. EVIDENCE-ONLY DISCOVERY CHAIN (owner-specified order, all identity-anchored): roster committed channelId → MB url-rel → **Wikidata P2397, VERIFIED Aug 21 as the exact property (label "YouTube channel ID"; empirically Q1299/The Beatles → UCc4K7bAqpdBP8jh1j9XZAww)**, reached only via the Q-id MB itself links from the confirmed MBID → **MB RECORDING url-rels** (a YouTube link on a recording in the confirmed artist's OWN catalog: the video is direct evidence for that exact title AND its channelId is the artist's channel by association — this is the sound reading of the owner's path (b), because joining the Discogs/gap-fill video sweep to a chart credit would require NAME equality, which discovery may not use). No evidence = no channel = honest null. GAP-FILL LINKING stays report-only for the same reason; the first candidates surfaced are instructive — Scott McKenzie (KH), The Jackson 5 (VN), The Sweet (TT) are the Phase-2 "regional pressing circuit" class (famous foreign acts pressed locally), i.e. exactly the false positives name-linking would have created. ⚠ SMOKE FINDING (23 artists, 4 min): the binding constraint is CHANNEL COVERAGE, not title matching — of 17 identity-confirmed artists only 2 (12%) had any YouTube channel in MB url-rels, so the rest are structurally unverifiable under the no-search rule (their catalogs live on auto-generated "- Topic" channels, which MB almost never links and which cannot be reached without a search). Verified samples are clean: Elvis "Jailhouse Rock", Paul Anka "Lonely Boy"/"Diana". Expect the ceiling to be era-dependent (modern artists carry official channels far more often); report.funnel splits identityUnconfirmed / confirmedWithoutChannel / confirmedWithChannel each night so the ceiling stays visible. JOINT-CREDIT CENSUS (owner asked for the scale before ruling; final, derived from the committed chart files): 2,934 rows / 2,654 unique keys / 1,513 distinct credited artists — **explicit-joint 322 rows (247 distinct credits: "featuring/feat./ft./vs/with/presents/meets/duet with/starring")**, **conjunction-ambiguous 288 rows (205 distinct: bare " & "/" and "/"+"/" x ", where the string ALONE cannot separate a duo's act-name from a joint credit — "Simon & Garfunkel" and "Adam and the Ants" are single acts, "Doris Day and Frankie Laine" is a joint credit)**, single 2,324 rows. NOTE the confirmed-MBID link rule dissolves most of the ambiguous class without a ruling: "Simon & Garfunkel" resolves to one MB artist and links; a true joint credit resolves to none and doesn't — evidence, not string parsing. A ruling is only needed if the owner wants joint credits to link to their *constituent* artists.
- **GAP-FILL CROSSWALK AUDIT COMPLETE — 13.7% MB-LINKED, AND ONLY 5.2% IS THE ACTUAL LEAK (Aug 25, 2026; REPORT ONLY, nothing in the dataset changed):** scripts/audit-gapfill-crosswalk.mjs finished all **15,381 id-carrying entries** (six resumable launches — two before this session, then an outage-lost window, an 8-min throughput probe, the finisher, and a 9-entry mop-up; work file and log gitignored, data/gapfill-crosswalk-report.json committed as ruling-round evidence). **2,102 entries are MB-linked = 13.7%**, against the 10.6% recorded at halfway — the gap is COMPOSITION, not drift, and the split proves it: the 54 countries checked first (the MB-invisible deep archive — LA, BT, TL, AF, MM, NP, MN, SD…) run 674/6,289 = **10.7%**, the 22 checked second (Caribbean, Levant/Gulf, post-Soviet, Balkans, plus the big C-batch pools CD/TT/GH/ZM) run 1,428/9,092 = **15.7%**. Leak rate is therefore an INVERSE PROXY FOR MB COVERAGE of a region, which is the reassuring direction: the dataset is cleanest exactly where it is the only source. Per-pool extremes make the same point — JO 34.3%, MD 33.4%, XK 31.9%, PS 31.4%, NI 23.5% at the top; LA **0/119 = 0.0%**, SD 1.8%, TL 3.1%, MM 4.8%, MN 5.8%, NP 6.1%, AF 7.7% at the bottom, plus zero hits in BT/TD/SB/VU. **byClass (2,109 matches over 2,102 entries — 7 Discogs pages carry two MB artists each): foreign-catalog 801 · unplaced 1,096 · local-dedup-miss 212.** WHAT EACH CLASS MEANS FOR THE RULING ROUND: (1) FOREIGN-CATALOG 801 is the real regional-pressing-circuit leak, and its origin spread is the finding — **125 distinct MB origin countries**, with US 98 (12.2%), CD 51, GB 50, CG 30, JM 24, FR 23, ES 21, EG 21, DE 19, KE 16, BE 15, ZA 15; the US+GB+FR+DE bloc is only 190 of 801 (23.7%), so this is overwhelmingly REGIONAL circulation (Congolese rumba across CD/CG, Jamaican repertoire across the Caribbean, Egyptian across the Levant/Gulf) rather than the Anglo-repertoire dumping the B3 pattern list led us to expect. (2) UNPLACED 1,096 — the largest class and the one that is NOT a verdict: MB carries no area at all, so the crosswalk proves identity and says nothing about origin; only **76 carry a beginArea** and 314 a disambiguation string, i.e. ~93% offer no machine-readable origin signal and cannot be auto-classified without violating lesson 5. (3) LOCAL-DEDUP-MISS 212 across 52 pools (CD 19, ET 16, GH 16, TT 13, CM 10, BJ 9, VN 9, KP 8) — genuine local acts MB knew about and the dedup failed to merge; these are the ones that were arguably never gap-fill material. CROSS-POOL SIGNAL PRESERVED: **103 Discogs ids surface in more than one pool**, the same shape that let the C ruling pass settle GY Jimmy James from TT's evidence — one ruling can now resolve several pools at once. METHOD, restated because it is what makes the evidence usable: MB url lookup on discogs.com/artist/{id} → linked MB artists → MB area vs pool country. **ID-level evidence end to end; no name matching anywhere**, so none of the romanization/homonym traps of lesson 3 apply. STATED LIMITATION (unchanged, in the report): **3,295 dataset entries carry no Discogs id and cannot be crosswalked at all** — coverage is 15,381 of 18,676 = 82.4%, and silence on the other 17.6% is absence of evidence, not evidence of absence. ECONOMICS, because the estimate was wrong: the handoff projected 3–7h for the back half; measured throughput was **4.53s/entry** (~4× the 1.1s pacing floor) making the true cost ~11h, and one full 420-min window was lost entirely to a network outage that cleared just 333 entries at ~76s/entry (11 hard failures, checkpoint lossless — the outage cost time, never data). The finishing launch raised --minutes 420→780; the deadline is a CAP not a duration (the loop exits when pending empties), so a longer cap can only avoid a needless extra window and a second 15-credit deploy. HELD-ROUND PRE-DERIVATION (lesson 8 — the queue was re-derived against current state, not inherited): data/pattern-ruling-report.json still proposes exactly **176 held**, all 176 keyed by a Discogs id, so all 176 are crosswalkable in principle. **74 of them now carry exact pool+id crosswalk evidence** — class split foreign-catalog 60 · unplaced 8 · local-dedup-miss 6 — and the identity check inside those 74 is the headline for the ruling round: **54 have their case MBID independently CONFIRMED** by the name-free URL path (the Aug-11 pass matched on names; the crosswalk reaches the same MBID through Discogs-page identity alone, so those identities now rest on two independent legs), while **20 point at a DIFFERENT MB artist than the case recorded** — i.e. ~27% of the evidenced held set may have been matched to the wrong MB artist and needs re-examination before any verdict, the lesson-1 shape (a verified name is not a verified claim). The remaining **102 held cases draw a genuine blank** — MB links nothing at all to their Discogs page, so the crosswalk cannot help them and they stay owner-judgment. UNBLOCKED, NOT STARTED: the held round + the Amr Diab tri-id are its own proposal, its own go, its own session.

ACCEPTED RESIDUAL, stated: pass-≤3 identities resting on weak corroboration are not re-checked; their videos passed channel+title+playability regardless, so exposure is identity metadata (10 roster links, all famous), not wrong videos. RESULTS: **188 artists newly confirmed** (identityConfirmed 911→1,092); verified videos 975→**1,105**, rows with ▶ 1,103→**1,254 of 2,934**; the Rolling Stones' 16 #1s — silently null on the live site because the "Fake"-stub ambiguity fired before verification — restored via the owner ruling, 11 with videos. ALIAS RELAXATION (owner-ruled Aug 23, measured Aug 24): 48 identities resolved via alias — 21 typed (Mr. Acker Bilk→Acker Bilk, John Cougar→John Mellencamp, Kanye West→Ye, The Singing Nun→Sœur Sourire), **27 via UNTYPED aliases, the relaxation's whole yield — and the flag list is CLEAN: every admission is a legitimate resolution** (U.S. Bonds→Gary "U.S." Bonds, The Timelords→The KLF, Janet→Janet Jackson, Paul McCartney and Wings→Wings, SSgt Barry Sadler→Barry Sadler, Little Jimmy Osmond→Jimmy Osmond, plus a block of and/& variants: Zager and Evans, Jan and Dean, Daryl Hall and John Oates, Sly and the Family Stone…). Nothing wrong let in; the catalogue-corroboration gate held. Note the and/& block partially dissolves the conjunction-ambiguous census class, as predicted by the confirmed-MBID link rule. AMBIGUITIES now carry song+year; 10 await the next ruling round (data/hits-play-report.json). CROSSWALK AUDIT AT HALFWAY: 6,266 of 15,381 checked, **666 MB-linked (10.6%)** — an order of magnitude beyond the hits-corpus estimate; full report lands after ~2 more nightly windows, then feeds the held-ruling round.
- **IDENTITY FIXES 1–5 + OWNER RULINGS ON THE 7 AMBIGUITIES (Aug 23, 2026, all approved and built):** (1) ALIAS-AWARE CANDIDATES — mbCandidates queries `artist:"X" OR alias:"X"` with an unquoted fallback (the phrase query `artist:"The Jackson 5"` returned ZERO results; MB files the band as "The Jacksons" with "The Jackson 5" as an alias) and accepts exact normalized equality on name OR a TYPED alias (Artist name / Legal name only, the Aug 8 rule). Search is candidate generation; equality + catalogue corroboration remain the gates, so a looser query cannot loosen identity. VERIFIED: The Jackson 5 → The Jacksons (e5257dc5), channel found via mb-rel. POLICY QUESTION SURFACED, NOT DECIDED: the real Sweet (dc73bb40, MB name "Sweet") carries "The Sweet" only as an UNTYPED alias, so the Aug 8 rule excludes it and "Blockbuster!" stays an honest null; allowing untyped aliases for candidate GENERATION (with catalogue corroboration still the gate — the Alexandra failure was an alias used as VERIFICATION) would recover this class. Owner's call. (2) CORROBORATION STRENGTH — recordingsFor() marks a recording STRONG only when it sits on a non-compilation release; confirmation requires ≥1 strong corroboration. "The Sweet" had been confirmed as a 2-track MB stub on ONE mis-credited Various-Artists compilation recording (nothing shipped — the stub had no channel — but the identity layer was fooled and only a downstream accident saved it); under fix 2 the stub no longer confirms. (3) PER-ROW IDENTITY + LIFESPAN TIEBREAKER — resolveRowIdentity() resolves per (credit, chart row): owner ruling → strong corroboration → eliminate candidates whose KNOWN lifespan excludes the chart year (unknown never eliminates, lesson 5) → prefer the sole candidate with a catalogue → else ambiguous (null + report, now with song + year). Channel discovery, uploads scan and matching run PER IDENTITY (record.identities). VERIFIED: Sweet Sensation resolves to TWO identities, one row each. OWNER RULINGS (Aug 23) live in lib/hits/identity-rulings.json (decisions in a file, lesson 2), applied before any search: The Firm → novelty group adecfd42 (supergroup ended 1986, chart 1987); The Elegants → 91919ad2 (1958, 6 RGs); The Manhattans → 8dce8a2d (NJ group, 31 RGs); Omi → Jamaican OMI 33e6ded6; Baccara → original lineup d17a5888 (Mayte Mateos' formed 1983); The Rolling Stones → b071f9fa (the namesake's MB disambiguation is literally "Fake"); Sweet Sensation → e8916411 for 1974 / 0d2ae2cd for 1990. All seven ambiguities were REAL MusicBrainz data (compilation releases mis-credited to same-name stubs — the "Fake" Stones on a bootleg called The BeatleStones, an Italian compilation crediting "Cheerleader" to an Israeli DJ), not a query bug; the gate flagged rather than guessed. (4) CROSSWALK-AS-LEAK-DETECTOR — scripts/audit-gapfill-crosswalk.mjs: for every gap-fill entry with a Discogs id, MB's url lookup (`/ws/2/url?resource=discogs.com/artist/{id}&inc=artist-rels`) names any MB artist linked to that exact page — ID-level evidence, no names — then MB's area classifies each hit foreign-catalog / local-dedup-miss / unplaced. Probe on the 6 hits candidates: 4 exact ID matches (Scott McKenzie KH, Enrique Iglesias PY, Edison Lighthouse NI=Nicaragua, Paul & Paula SR — all the regional-pressing-circuit class), i.e. two independent databases agreeing on a number. OWNER RULING: evidence for the held-ruling round, never links. 15,381 of 18,676 entries carry a Discogs id (3,295 id-less cannot be crosswalked); report → data/gapfill-crosswalk-report.json (committed). HAZARD CAUGHT IN SMOKE: under load MB answered 200 WITHOUT a relations key — recorded naively that is a silent false negative; the script now treats a missing key as transient and retries. (5) REPORT DEDUP — ambiguous/gap-fill lists accumulated across passes (The Firm listed twice, 11 gap-fill rows for 6 candidates); assemble() dedupes by content. THUMBNAILS (owner agreed: not for identity) — automatic face recognition of real people will not be built; album-art matching has no free reliable path and fails on lyric videos / Topic art / tribute acts reusing originals' covers. LOGGED FOR LATER: a VISUAL REVIEW CARD for the spot-check protocol — thumbnail + channel + duration + chart row on one card so a human confirms a picked video in seconds; i.ytimg.com thumbnails cost zero quota. PASS bumped to 4 (full identity re-sweep; verified rows skipped).
- **HITS SWEEP — PASS 3 SUBSTANTIALLY COMPLETE (Aug 23, 2026):** 1,310 of 1,513 credited artists swept under recording-level corroboration with the duration floor, non-song blocklist and studio preference all active from the start. **975 verified videos · 1,103 of 2,934 chart rows carry a ▶ · 50.5% match rate.** Funnel: 911 identity-confirmed of 1,341 swept, 518 of those with a channel. Channel discovery final shape — mb-rel 444, **wikidata 56**, roster 10, mb-recording 8; 22 Topic channels, all evidence-reached, zero discovered by name. Verified via uploads 950 / mb-recording 25. Live-labelled 41 (down from 168 pre-studio-preference). Roster links 10 artists / 85 rows, all on confirmed-MBID equality. 8 ambiguous identities and 11 gap-fill candidates sit in data/hits-play-report.json for an owner ruling round. REMAINING: 203 artists, deferred mid-window because MusicBrainz degraded to 1-of-3 healthy probes — resume with `node scripts/build-hits-play.mjs` (resumable, pass 3), no re-match needed for them since the filters now run from the start.
- **KNOWN RESIDUALS — HITS PLAY LINKS (owner-ruled Aug 22, 2026: leave both, keep them visible, do NOT chase them):** two classes survive every filter and are accepted rather than hidden. (1) TRUNCATED/ABBREVIATED UPLOADS — an upload long enough to clear the 90s floor but shorter than the real recording. Worked case: Blondie "Call Me" resolves to a 135s upload while MusicBrainz's shortest legitimate recording of it is 175s (its lengths run 175–488s across edits/remixes/live plus a junk 3s entry). The only real fix is cross-checking video duration against MB recording lengths, which needs outlier filtering and a generous tolerance and would false-reject official videos with long intros — OWNER RULING: "not worth it for a handful of rows", do not build it. (2) LIVE TAKES NAMED AFTER A CONCERT FILM rather than annotated "live" — worked case: Kylie Minogue "Slow (INFINITE DISCO)", a livestream concert performance that carries no live marker. Adding individual concert names is whack-a-mole; OWNER RULING: leave it visible. Both are the predicted shape of standing lesson 7's blind spot: a second check on WHAT a thing is can bound the error but never eliminate it.
- **SPOT-CHECK → DURATION FLOOR + NON-SONG BLOCKLIST + RE-MATCH PASS (owner-commissioned audit Aug 22, 2026; all three approved and shipped):** the owner asked for ~15 random newly-verified rows to be confirmed as genuinely that song by that artist. VERIFICATION METHOD MATTERED: checking against the sweep's own stored titles would have been circular, so each video's REAL metadata was pulled from YouTube (title, channel, duration) and compared to the chart row independently. RESULT — NOT CLEAN: 10 correct, **2 outright wrong** (Madonna "Who's That Girl" → the FILM TRAILER, 1:06; Chuck Berry "My Ding-a-Ling" → a 25-second clip), 3 flagged (Kylie "Slow" → an INFINITE DISCO concert take not tagged live; Bon Jovi → a Letterman take although the studio upload sat on the same channel; Blondie "Call Me" → 2:15 vs the single's 3:32). A follow-up scan of ALL 691 verified rows measured the true scale: **55 suspect (8.0%)** — 40 under 90 seconds (Kylie's 9-second "🎡 Spinning Around 🎡", Craig David's 12-second Short) and 17 non-song titles (Behind The Scenes ×3, Book Trailer, Documentary Music Video). ROOT CAUSE = standing lesson 7. FIXES: (1) MIN_DURATION_SECONDS 90 with no ceiling, enforced inside videoFacts (which now fetches contentDetails at the same 1-unit cost); (2) NON_SONG_MARKERS rejected in matchUpload, tested against the ANNOTATION only so a song whose own title contains the word is unaffected; (3) `--phase rematch` — YouTube-only so it never competes with a sweep — re-picking every already-verified row under the new rules PLUS the studio preference, which had never been applied because it landed after pass 3 had already picked first-match-wins. RE-MATCH RESULTS: 38 rows dropped to honest nulls, **162 swapped to a better pick**, verified 691→653, rows with ▶ 789→747, match rate 52.2%→49.4% (the drop is the point — wrong matches left). Live labels fell 168→24 because the studio preference found real alternatives. Every spot-check defect resolved: Madonna's trailer REPLACED by the actual Official Video, Paula Abdul's Behind-The-Scenes replaced by "Rush, Rush", Cher Lloyd's replaced by the Official Video, the Shorts dropped, Bon Jovi now studio. STILL OPEN (reported, not hidden): Blondie "Call Me" 135s passes the floor (lesson 7's stated blind spot), and Kylie's "Slow (INFINITE DISCO)" stays untagged because that concert-film name is not a live marker — chasing individual concert names would be whack-a-mole, so it is left as the acknowledged residual.
- **LIVE-VERSION HANDLING (owner ruling Aug 21, 2026: prefer studio, accept live rather than nothing, label it honestly):** implemented after reporting the signals and the residual error first, per the owner's "tell me before building it if this is harder than it sounds". THE KEY INSIGHT THAT MAKES IT SAFE: the live marker is tested against the ANNOTATION ONLY — what remains of the upload title after the charting title (and any "Artist - " prefix) is removed — never the whole string. A naive /live/ test would mislabel every song whose own title contains the word; scoping to the annotation means "Live and Let Die (Official Video)" keeps "Live" in the MATCHED portion and correctly reads studio, while "Live Forever (Live at Knebworth 1996)" reads live. matchUpload now collects ALL matching uploads and returns `matches.find(m => !m.live) ?? matches[0]` — studio preferred, live accepted rather than leaving the row silent. Markers: live / unplugged / in concert / en vivo / en directo / live lounge / bbc session / session at / acoustic session. TESTED 11/11 against the adversarial set (Live and Let Die, Live Forever both ways, Livin' on a Prayer, Alive, MTV Unplugged, Live Aid, bare "Yesterday"); test harness extracts the REAL functions from the script by line-anchored slicing rather than testing a copy. KNOWN RESIDUAL ERROR, REPORTED BEFORE BUILDING: a live recording uploaded with NO annotation is indistinguishable from the studio record and will be treated as studio — false positives near zero, false negatives are this class. SERVING: hits-play.json entries gain `live: true`, HitEntry carries `live`, the row renders a small "live" tag beside the title and the Play-all queue labels the track "(artist) (live)". The flag is DERIVED AT ASSEMBLE TIME from the stored upload title, so rows verified under earlier passes get labelled without a re-sweep. ALSO FIXED (data-loss hazard found while wiring this): `--assemble` used loadJson's fallback, so a work file caught mid-write (the sweep saves after every artist) parsed as null and would have OVERWRITTEN the committed hits-play.json with `{}`; it now throws instead — durable data outlives code, refuse rather than truncate.
- **CORROBORATION GATE FIXED — THE REAL CEILING (owner-approved Aug 21, 2026, in the order 3→2→1):** the depth cap was a red herring; the binding constraint was the corroboration gate itself. PROBE FIRST (owner instruction — confirm before building): The Beatles' MBID, 94 release-group titles vs their 24 unmatched chart titles → **23 of 24 were ABSENT from release-group titles**, so they were written off as null WITHOUT EVER being compared against the channel; yet **6 of 8 sampled blocked titles exist as RECORDINGS in the same MBID's catalog**. Singles are frequently not release groups. FIX (approved item 2): corroboration now falls through release groups → `arid:{mbid} AND recording:"{title}"` exact-title recordings. This COMPLETES the John Mayer protection rather than weakening it — the query is anchored to the artist's own MBID, so identity is never inferred from a name; ambiguity (two same-name candidates both corroborating) still resolves to null. Identity resolution also gained the recording fallback on a bounded sample, so artists whose singles never became release groups can now be confirmed at all. MEASURED ON THE PROBE CASE: The Beatles went **1 → 26 verified of 41 chart rows (+25)**, and every recovery came via `uploads` — i.e. the videos were on their channel the whole time, blocked solely by the gate. DOUBLE A-SIDES also handled: a row like "Come Together / Something" is two songs, so `titleSides()` corroborates and matches per side as well as whole (the 2 of 8 probe titles that failed recording corroboration were exactly these). DEPTH CAP (approved item 1, deprioritized as predicted): UPLOAD_PAGES 10 → 60, paging still stops naturally at nextPageToken so only the giants pay; measured cost of full scans across every over-500-video channel ≈350 units and ~2 min wall-clock, expected recovery only ~10–20 titles (full-scan probe: Beatles 0 recoverable by depth, Elvis 5 — "Good Luck Charm"/"Surrender"/"Way Down" sat on pages 14–15). SWEEP PASS bumped to 3 (re-sweeps everything; already-verified rows are skipped, so only nulls are retried). NEW `--only "<credit>"` flag for targeted single-artist verification. COST ACCEPTED BY OWNER: +1 MB call per uncorroborated row (~1 hour of extra wall-clock on the backlog) — "correctness beats speed here". NOTE for review: the exact-match decoration strip means a title can resolve to an official LIVE version when that is what the channel hosts (Beatles "All You Need Is Love" → a Live-from upload) — right artist, right song, official channel, playable; flagged as a judgment call, not a defect.
- **STALE-LINK ASSESSMENT — MB url-rels across the play chain (owner-commissioned Aug 21, 2026; REPORT ONLY, nothing fixed):** commissioned after the hits sweep found Pharrell's MB-linked channel (PharrellWilliamsTV) genuinely lacks "Happy". THE HYPOTHESIS WAS LARGELY WRONG AND THE CORRECTION MATTERS: MB url-rels are NOT stale. Measured over all 310 channels the sweep discovered — **0 DEAD/REMOVED (0.0%)** — and mb-rel channels carry a 69.2% title-match rate with only 16.6% zero-yield (Wikidata-sourced 76.9%/18.8%). There is no broken-link problem. THE ACTUAL PHENOMENON is "right artist, WRONG CONTENT HOME": the channel is legitimate, current, and correctly identified, but is not where the catalog lives — 34 channels (11.0%) hold ≤10 videos and 98 (31.6%) hold ≤50, because the recordings live on VEVO/Topic channels instead. Michael Jackson's official channel is the clean proof: 19 videos total, 1 of his 17 chart titles found; Ini Kamoze 2 videos, DJ Casper 2. BLAST RADIUS IS NARROW — it splits by whether a surface needs a PAGE or a TRACK: (a) PAGE-LEVEL, UNAFFECTED — the play chain's MB step emits kinds 'youtube-channel'/'bandcamp'/'soundcloud'/'official' labeled "Artist's YouTube channel" etc. (lib/play/types.ts PLAY_LABELS); a 19-video MJ channel IS Michael Jackson's channel, so the label's promise is kept. Panel ▶, Discover (same chain), /api/explore/artist-links, and artist pages (owner-curated channelIds + oEmbed-verified video IDs) are all page-level and sound. (b) TRACK-LEVEL, AFFECTED — the queue resolver (/api/queue/artist/[mbid]/[decade]) needs a specific era-correct song present on the channel and shares the hits sweep's exact failure mode; it is ALREADY mitigated by the corroborated-search fallback the John Mayer ruling built, which the hits sweep deliberately lacks (owner banned search there). CONCLUSION: not a site-wide defect, one shared failure mode on one surface that already carries a mitigation. NOTE ALSO the roster channels' low 11.4% title-rate is a SCAN-DEPTH artifact, not a data problem — The Beatles' channel holds 524 videos against the sweep's 500-video (10-page) cap, and the roster's chart artists average 12 hits each. NO FIXES APPLIED per owner instruction.
- **UK NATIONS ON THE GLOBE (Feature 2):** England/Scotland/Wales/Northern Ireland clickable exactly like US states — same zoom-decides swap (enter <1.15 / exit >1.3), zoomed-out UK unchanged by construction. GEO: public/data/uk-nations-110m.geojson — Natural Earth 110m admin-0 map units (public domain), 4 features, 2KB, props {ADMIN, ISO_A2: GB-ENG/SCT/WLS/NIR}; SEAMLESS with the countries layer because NE map units tile exactly into the same dataset's GB outline (and the NI–Ireland land border stays resolution-consistent). PRECOMPUTE: scripts/build-state-data.mjs generalized with --region us|uk (per-region config/work/output paths; US defaults unchanged); config lib/explore/uk-nations.json (~170 curated names, page overrides England/London 40). WALK-RULE CHANGE (critical for UK): resolveArea now stops ONLY at ISO-3166-1 country codes and walks THROUGH non-target ISO-3166-2 codes — UK cities/counties carry their own codes (Manchester GB-MAN, Glasgow GB-GLG, Essex GB-ESS) which under the old rule dead-ended every walk below nation level; foreign subdivisions climb to their country and resolve null there (US semantics unchanged in practice). PLUMBING: lib/explore/states.ts is now the subdivided-regions module (US_STATES + UK_NATIONS, SUBDIVIDED_REGIONS, REGION_CODE_PATTERN /^(?:US-[A-Z]{2}|GB-[A-Z]{3})$/, regionByCode/regionByName incl. aliases Cymru/Ulster); geo.ts featureCode + ExploreClient deep-link regex accept 3-letter suffixes; GlobeScene loads both geojsons + both count files lazily at the swap (nationCountsRef; heat normalized WITHIN each country's group — US states and UK nations share no scale), tooltip + fly-to + search-list "UK · England" insertion generalized; search route resolves nation names/aliases registry-first and area walks now return GB-ENG-class codes (Manchester→England); surprise landings draw from all 55 regions; /api/explore serves regions from the merged committed dataset (lib/explore/uk-nation-artists.json) with the Hawaii-style live-MB fallback until data lands. SMOKE (GB-WLS+GB-NIR, verified): Wales 2,551 artists on record (top5 Manic Street Preachers, MARINA, Budgie, Super Furry Animals, Los Campesinos!), NI 1,580 (Gary Moore, Van Morrison, Therapy?, The Undertones, Ash); areaCache asserts Cardiff→GB-WLS, Belfast→GB-NIR. FULL RUN (Aug 20, completed same evening — the smoke's checkpoint carried over, single launch despite MB throttling every request with one 503): 37,533 artists assigned — GB-ENG 28,009 on record (top5 Pink Floyd, The Beatles, Queen, The Cure, Aphex Twin), GB-SCT 5,388 (Boards of Canada, SOPHIE, Mogwai, Cocteau Twins, Franz Ferdinand), GB-WLS 2,556 (Manic Street Preachers, MARINA, Budgie, Super Furry Animals, Los Campesinos!), GB-NIR 1,580 (Van Morrison, Gary Moore, Therapy?, The Divine Comedy, Calibre); dataset 398KB committed. ALL FOUR owner-specified asserts pass against the area cache: Manchester→GB-ENG, Glasgow→GB-SCT, Cardiff→GB-WLS, Belfast→GB-NIR. Panels verified from committed data (instant, MB-free): England 1990, Scotland 1980–99, Wales 1995 (Manics/SFA/Catatonia — Cool Cymru era-correct), NI 1970 (Van Morrison, Them, James Galway). Bundle-leak scan clean: neither uk-nation-artists.json nor the hits datasets appear in .next/static. OPERATIONAL NOTE: MusicBrainz 503-stormed then connection-timed-out when the precompute script and live panel queries (dev-server browser testing) ran concurrently from one IP — sequence MB consumers, never overlap them; capped relaunch loops (10–40 × 60s) are the standard wrapper here.

### Standing lessons (owner-mandated, Aug 8, 2026 — do not relearn these)

1. **Verifying the ARTIST is not verifying the CLAIM about the artist.** Discover checked Butcher Brown existed on MusicBrainz, then shipped a fabricated album title ("Camp Culture") beside his verified name. Every field a model emits needs its own verification against the source of truth, not a halo from the fields that were checked.
2. **Fixing a generator does not fix what the generator already wrote.** Cached pools, Blobs queues, snapshot data, and committed JSON all outlive the code that produced them — each needs its own sanitization pass or render-time guard. The Alexandra badge and the stored Following search-hrefs were both found only on LIVE, after the generator fix deployed clean locally.
3. **Archival identity matching (owner method, Aug 8, 2026):** Never match non-Latin artist names on script similarity. Match on romanization PLUS at least one independent corroborating fact: shared collaborator, shared song title, shared label, or shared era. Script spelling of pre-digital records varies by transcriber; romanization and collaborators do not. (Worked example: ກ. ວິເສດ / ກ. ວຶເສສ / ກ. ວິເສສ all romanize to K. Viseth, and the Discogs duet credit with ນາງສາວຮະບຽບ matches Radiodiffusion's independently documented duet partner Nang Sao Rabiap — see the note on dg:4897853 in lib/play/archival-links.json.)
4. **Rule on the record, not the query (owner ruling, Aug 9, 2026).** Discogs's country search returned ~30% different releases two days apart, yet a per-release audit of ALL 328 departed releases found every single one still carrying the correct country field — zero corrections, zero deletions. Query layers (search indexes, display strings) lie; per-record fields are the truth. Drift-dropped artists are therefore RETAINED permanently (retain-drift-dropped.mjs, retainedFrom-marked); a future departure is presumed flaky until audit-drift-departures.mjs shows its country field actually changed.
5. **Absent values must never satisfy an identity or verification comparison. Missing is not a match.** Three instances in ONE day (Aug 8, 2026): the queue route's ASCII normalize reduced non-Latin names and channels to '' and '' === '' passed the official-channel bar; a cache read served a stored null ahead of a committed verified video; the split-dedup's id comparison treated null === null as "already present." Every equality gate needs an explicit both-sides-present check before the comparison means anything.
6. **Absence-in-a-haystack is not verification (Aug 17, 2026 — the Netlify bandwidth incident).** Deploy-verify pollers that required famous excluded names to be ABSENT from all site chunks could never exit — same-name different-artist entries and prose data legitimately contain those strings — and four orphaned loops silently transferred ~46GB (~93% of the month's bandwidth budget) re-downloading every chunk uncompressed every ~60s for days. Background pollers need: POSITIVE markers only, a hard iteration cap, compressed single-asset requests (HEAD-etag until change, then ONE targeted fetch), and a completion notification. Audit for orphaned pollers at the START of every session.

7. **A title match verifies the NAME of a thing, never WHAT it is (owner-mandated, Aug 22, 2026).** A 9-second Short, a movie trailer, a behind-the-scenes reel and the actual record all carry the song's exact title — so exact-title matching, however strict, cannot tell them apart. The hits sweep shipped 8% bad play buttons this way (55 of 691 rows): Madonna's "Who's That Girl" resolved to the FILM TRAILER, Chuck Berry's "My Ding-a-Ling" to a 25-second clip, Kylie's "Spinning Around" to a 9-second Short, and "(Behind The Scenes)" reels passed because the decoration-strip that correctly removes "(Official Video)" removes those too. ANY match on a title needs a SECOND check that the artifact is the kind of thing claimed — duration, media type, or the annotation that says what it is. Encoded as MIN_DURATION_SECONDS (90, no ceiling — official videos legitimately run long) plus NON_SONG_MARKERS tested against the annotation only. KNOWN BLIND SPOT, stated rather than assumed: a floor kills Shorts and clips but passes truncated or abbreviated uploads — Blondie's "Call Me" resolves to a 135s upload when MB's shortest legitimate recording of it is 175s, and no length rule short of cross-checking MB recording lengths would catch it (MB's own lengths are noisy: a junk 3s entry, and 175–488s spanning edits, remixes and live takes).

8. **An escalation queue is derived data — re-derive it before handing it over (owner-mandated, Aug 24, 2026).** The hits sweep surfaced 10 "open" ambiguities when 7 were already resolved by the owner's own rulings from the day before — the staleness check was outsourced to the owner's attention. Before surfacing anything as "waiting on the owner," re-verify every item against CURRENT state: rulings files, the dataset as it stands now, a live probe of the claimed blocker. The test for what is genuinely the owner's: what would they DO with the item first? A lookup Claude could run means the item isn't ready — run it, then resolve-and-report or attach the result so the owner's first move is judgment. Judgment the data cannot supply — policy, taste, balanced evidence, money, rights, irreversibles, or an explicitly reserved class (person-lifespan ties, the archival-source allowlist, origin rulings) — is the owner's, and resolving it unilaterally is the mirror failure. Resolutions are REPORTED, never silent: the "already covered" lines are themselves the audit of the resolution. Queues this applies to on sight: the held-ruling cases, the gap-fill candidate lists, the STEFANO ACTION QUEUE, any "pending owner" flag older than the state it depends on.

### Decisions made
- No dependencies beyond Next.js: plain CSS Modules, `next/font` (Fraunces display serif + Inter), no Tailwind/zod/test framework (adding any requires Stefano's OK).
- Embeds stored as `{type, kind, id}` — components build official embed URLs. ~~Audio files are structurally inexpressible~~ SUPERSEDED July 14, 2026: the `play` schema section hosts audio for artists whose masters the site has explicit rights to (owner-provided only; validator requires a rights statement) — see NATIVE AUDIO v1 entry.
- All iframes are click-to-load facades (performance); YouTube facades use i.ytimg.com thumbnails.
- `shows.upcoming` is EMPTY on purpose: no official 2026 dates exist (W.A.S.T.E. schedule + Ticketmaster both empty as of July 2026). A fan/tribute page ("Just Radiohead") and an AI-clickbait site (aesplora.com) publish fake "2026 tour announcements" — do not import them. Only trust wasteheadquarters.com.
- Hero/merch images are local SVG placeholders — no rights-cleared band photos; Spotify API artwork replaces them at milestone 5.
- `next.config.ts` has `allowedDevOrigins: ['127.0.0.1']` — dev-only fix: Next 16 blocks cross-origin dev resources and the preview browser hits 127.0.0.1, which silently breaks hydration (page renders but clicks do nothing). Not needed in production.

- DEDUP RULE v3 + RETROACTIVE PASS (Aug 9, 2026, owner-approved all six points): scripts/lib/dedup-rule.mjs is now THE dedup — (1) fuzzy NEVER drops (near-miss log only); (2) a name match alone never drops — dropping needs record-level corroboration (area via MB AREA HIERARCHY walk, era overlap, shared release title, or Wikidata crosswalk); (3) area contradiction triggers the shared-release-title discriminator — titles overlap = same artist but FOREIGN (verdict 'foreign-catalog', an ORIGIN exclusion logged separately from dedup); no overlap = collision, candidate KEPT; (4) MB aliases count only typed Artist/Legal name, candidate ANVs probe only multi-token (bare forenames decide nothing — the Amambay bug); (5) verdicts persist mbid+basis (index-weather-proof); (6) retro pass over all 338 v2 drops via scripts/apply-dedup-rule-v3.mjs (seeded with audited MBIDs — judged against RECORDS, searched only for irreproducibles). RESULTS: 70 duplicates upheld, 94 foreign-catalog exclusions (Beatles/Queen/Madonna class — confirmed OUT, now for the origin reason), 43 fuzzy-kept + 89 collision-kept + 41 uncorroborated-kept → net 150 reinstated incl. Amambay Cardozo Ocampo (verified play link intact) and Víctor Hugo De La Sierra (his MB 'match' was the FRENCH POET via partial ANV). - VETTED ARCHIVAL TIER + ROUND-3 DIRECTIVES (Aug 8, 2026, owner-approved design): NEW play tier for artists whose recordings predate YouTube — lib/play/archival-sources.json (allowlist of legitimate archives: channelId + evidenceUrl + addedBy/addedOn; OWNER-EDITED ONLY, no code path writes it) + lib/play/archival-links.json (owner-curated playKey → {videoId, sourceChannelId, label}). THE GUARD RAILS: the allowlist authorizes SOURCES, never matches artists; automation may VALIDATE (scripts/validate-archival.mjs: playability + channel-membership, exit 1 on failure — run before committing changes) but never DISCOVER — no name/title matching populates mappings, so there is no matcher to fool. Resolves FIRST in /api/play (human judgment outranks automation); renders as kind 'archival-video', label "Watch on YouTube — vetted archive"; defense-in-depth: a mapping whose channel leaves the allowlist stops rendering. - LINK RULINGS ROUND 2 — YT SEARCH BANNED SITE-WIDE, SERVICE SEARCHES RELABELED, FUZZY MATCHING BANNED (Aug 8, 2026, owner rulings after live verification): (2a) YouTube search URLs are GONE from the codebase — `listenSearch`/`quotedSearch`/`youtubeSearchUrl` deleted from lib/links.ts and lib/explore/panelData.ts, zero call sites remain (a YouTube miss lands on unrelated video content; that's the brand damage). (2b) Music-service searches KEPT with honest labels: lib/listen/services.ts now exports musicServiceSearchUrl + SEARCH_LABELS ("Search on Spotify" etc.); resolveListenHref returns {href, service, label, rerouted}, never YouTube — YouTube-service fans reroute to Apple Music at release level; ▶ is reserved for links that actually play. Relabeled surfaces: AlbumCard, CatalogPopup, tracklists, FeedPostCard, feed upcoming strip, ArtistEraPanel, WhatWasPlaying, GenreStory prose mentions (Apple Music artist search), story-card media. (2c) Release-level YouTube = verified video ID or the service link — feed videos/queue keep IDs, everything else searches a music service. Fixed: script-aware normalize, empty-name guards, and a v2 queue-cache keyspace scoped to the vulnerable class (Latin-name cache entries untouched); queueCacheKey is shared from lib/play/resolve.ts so the play chain's step-0 peek matches. (Finding B, accepted): alias-set EXACT matching — MB aliases ride the url-rels call (inc=url-rels+aliases), Discogs "A = B" credits split into script variants, Wikidata labels in the sweep; IA queries run on up to two spellings and a creator must exact-match an alias. More spellings, never looser matching; play cache bumped to v3 so non-Latin nulls retry with romanizations.
